\displaystyle=\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}+\tau(\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime})]\big|_{\tau=0}-\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}+\tau(\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime})]\big|_{\tau=1}

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{d\tau}\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}+\tau(\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime})]\big|_{\tau=\tau^{\ast}}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime}).

Moreover, using (
42
)-(
43
) we get

\left\lVert\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]-\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(U^{k})}\leq\frac{\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}}{1-\max\{\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})},\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}\}}\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}.

Therefore, combining all these calculations we have proved that

(59)

\left\lVert D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\mathsf{h}-D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\mathsf{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(U^{k})}\lesssim\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}.

On the other hand, taking into account Remark
3.6
and proceeding as before, we have that

\left\lVert D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\mathsf{h}-D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\mathsf{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))}=\left\lVert\frac{\left(\varpi^{\prime}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)\left(\mathsf{h}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}{1+\left(\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))}\\
+\left\lVert\frac{\left(\varpi^{\prime}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\right)\left(\mathsf{h}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\right)}{1+\left(\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\right)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V^{-}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))},

where we have used the disjoint union given by

V(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))=V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))\cup V^{-}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon)),

with

\displaystyle V^{+}(z):=\{(r,\theta)\in V(z):\mathrm{m}(z,\theta)=\Phi_{1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}](z,\theta)\},

\displaystyle V^{-}(z):=\{(r,\theta)\in V(z):\mathrm{M}(z,\theta)=\Phi_{1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}](z,\theta)\},

for
z\in\left\{R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon:i,j=1,2\right\}
.

Since
\varpi^{\prime}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\equiv 0
over
V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon)
by definition, we directly have that

\left\lVert\frac{\left(\varpi^{\prime}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)\left(\mathsf{h}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}{1+\left(\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))}\\
=\left\lVert\frac{\left(\varpi^{\prime}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]-\varpi^{\prime}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\right)\left(\mathsf{h}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}{1+\left(\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))}.

Now, using the fact that Hölder space
C^{0,\alpha}(V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))
is a Banach algebra together with the analogous of (
58
) we get

\left\lVert\frac{\left(\varpi^{\prime}\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)\left(\mathsf{h}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}{1+\left(\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}^{\prime}\circ\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\right)}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))}\\
\leq\left\lVert\varpi^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}\left\lVert\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]-\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V^{+}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))}\frac{\left\lVert\mathsf{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}}{1-\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}}\\
\leq\left\lVert\varpi^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}\frac{\left\lVert\mathsf{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}}{1-\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{1,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}}.

Proceeding similarly with straightforward modifications we can conclude the same result over the domain
V^{-}(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon)
. As a immediate consequence, combining both, we have proved that

(60)

\left\lVert D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\mathsf{h}-D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\mathsf{h}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(V(R_{i}+(-1)^{j}\varepsilon))}\lesssim\left\lVert\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\right\rVert_{C^{0,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}.

In summary, by putting together (
57
), (
59
) and (
60
) we have obtained (
56
). So, backing up our argument, we have proved our initial objective (
50
), i.e.,

\|D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda,\mathsf{f}^{\prime}]\mathsf{h}-D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda,\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}]\mathsf{h}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}\lesssim\|\mathsf{f}^{\prime}-\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})},

for any pair
\mathsf{f}^{\prime},\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{B}_{\delta}(X(D_{\varepsilon}))
and
\mathsf{h}\in X(D_{\varepsilon}).

Consequently, we have obtained that the Gâteaux derivatives are continuous
with respect to the strong topology and hence they are, in fact, Fréchet derivatives. Therefore, we can conclude that the Fréchet derivative exists and coincides with the Gâteaux derivative. See
[
27
,
33
]
for more details.
∎

3.2.
An explicit expression for the differential at the origin

Recall that the linearization of (
18
) around
\mathsf{f}=0,
thanks to the expression (
28
), is given by

D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda,0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\lambda r\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)+D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)\\
-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\lambda r\mathsf{h}(r,s)+D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[0]\mathsf{h}(r,s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Evaluating (
32
) at
\mathsf{f}=0
we have

D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=D_{\mathsf{f}}\psi[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)+\partial_{r}\psi[0](r,\theta)\mathsf{h}(r,\theta).

Recall that
\psi[0]
is the solution of (
30
)-(
31
) for
\mathsf{f}=0
. In this particular case, the system is reduced to a system that depends only on the radial variable. That is,
\psi[0](r,\theta)\equiv\psi[0](r)
solving

\left\{\begin{aligned} -\left(\psi[0]^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{1}{r}\psi[0]^{\prime}(r)\right)&=\varpi(r),\qquad r\in[r_{1},r_{2}],\\
\psi[0](r_{1})&=0,\\
\psi[0](r_{2})&=\gamma,\end{aligned}\right.

Likewise, we recall that
D_{\mathsf{f}}\psi[0]\mathsf{h}
is the solution of (
34
)-(
35
) for
\mathsf{f}=0
. That is,

\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta_{B}\left(D_{\mathsf{f}}\psi[0]\mathsf{h}\right)(r,\theta)&=D_{\mathsf{f}}\omega[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta),\qquad(r,\theta)\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}},\\
D_{\mathsf{f}}\psi[0]\mathsf{h}(r_{1},\theta)&=0,\\
D_{\mathsf{f}}\psi[0]\mathsf{h}(r_{2},\theta)&=0,\end{aligned}\right.

with

D_{\mathsf{f}}\omega[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\begin{cases}0\qquad&r_{1}\leq r<R_{1}-\varepsilon,\\
-\varpi^{\prime}(r)\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)\qquad&R_{1}-\varepsilon\leq r\leq R_{1}+\varepsilon,\\
0&R_{1}+\varepsilon<r<R_{2}-\varepsilon,\\
-\varpi^{\prime}(r)\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)\qquad&R_{2}-\varepsilon\leq r\leq R_{2}+\varepsilon,\\
0&R_{2}+\varepsilon<r\leq r_{2}.\end{cases}

Since
\mathsf{h}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
, in particular
\mathsf{h}
is mean zero in the angular variable. Then, we can deduce that
D_{\mathsf{f}}\omega[0]\mathsf{h}
is also mean zero in the angular variable and consequently also

D_{\mathsf{f}}\psi[0]\mathsf{h}
. For this reason, we can conclude that
\int_{\mathbb{T}}\lambda r\mathsf{h}(r,s)+D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[0]\mathsf{h}(r,s)\,\mbox{d}s=0
and finally the linearization around
\mathsf{f}=0
is simply

D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda,0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\lambda r\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)+D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta).

Up to this point we have proved Hypothesis 1 and 2 of C-R theorem
3.1
. The bulk and main task of the manuscript will be to prove Hypothesis 3. In order to do that, we need an explicit and manageable expression of the linear operator.

4.
Analysis of the linear operator

Let
\mathsf{h}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
with expansion

\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}.

Above, see Section
3.2
, we have proved that

D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda,0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\lambda r\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)+D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta),

with

D_{\mathsf{f}}\bar{\psi}[0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\phi^{\prime}(r)\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)+\Phi(r,\theta),

where
\phi\equiv\phi(r)
solves

(61)

\left\{\begin{aligned} -\left(\phi^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{1}{r}\phi^{\prime}(r)\right)&=\varpi(r),\qquad r\in[r_{1},r_{2}],\\
\phi(r_{1})&=0,\\
\phi(r_{2})&=\gamma,\end{aligned}\right.

and
\Phi\equiv\Phi(r,\theta)
solves

(62)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta_{B}\Phi(r,\theta)&=\varpi^{\prime}(r)h(r,\theta),\qquad(\rho,\theta)\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\\
\Phi(r_{1},\theta)&=0,\\
\Phi(r_{2},\theta)&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Here, with a little abuse of notation, since
\supp(\varpi^{\prime})=I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}
we will understand that

\varpi^{\prime}(r)h(r,\theta)=\begin{cases}\varpi^{\prime}(r)h(r,\theta),\qquad&(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon},\\
0,&(r,\theta)\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}.\end{cases}

Notice that
\mathsf{h}
is not defined in
\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\setminus D_{\varepsilon}.

4.1.
Solving the boundary value problems (
61
) and (
62
)

For general
\varpi
, the solution of (
61
) is given by

(63)

\phi(r)=C_{\varpi}\log\left(\frac{r}{r_{1}}\right)-\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi(t)\,\mbox{d}t\right)\,\mbox{d}s,

with

(64)

C_{\varpi}=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi(t)\,\mbox{d}t\right)\,\mbox{d}s\right].

Similarly, we also need an explicit expression for the system (
62
). In order to do that, taking the Fourier transform in the angular variable and using the expansion

(65)

\Phi(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\Phi_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),

we have (see Appendix
11.2
) that

(66)

\Phi_{n}(r)=\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\varpi^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)h_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\varpi^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)h_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

where we have introduced the auxiliary hyperbolic functions

(67)

\mathcal{S}_{n}(r):=\sinh(n\log r),\qquad\mathcal{C}_{n}(r):=\cosh(n\log r).

Combining all, we immediately obtain that

D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda,0]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda r)\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)+\Phi(r,\theta),\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon},

with
\Phi
given by (
65
)-(
66
) and
\phi
given by (
63
)-(
64
). Then, defining
\mathcal{L}[\lambda]\mathsf{h}:=D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda,0]\mathsf{h}
give us that

(68)

\mathcal{L}[\lambda]\mathsf{h}=\mathcal{L}[\lambda]\left(\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta)\right)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]\Pi_{n}\mathsf{h}

where
\Pi_{n}
is just the projector onto
\cos(n\theta)
and
\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]
is the operator given by

(69)

\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]\mathsf{g}(r):=\left(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda r\right)\mathsf{g}(r)\\
+\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\varpi^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s-\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\varpi^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}.

4.2.
Introducing the Taylor-Couette profile

Up to this moment we have been working during all this section for a general circular flow. However, we are just interested in the dynamics around the particular case of the Taylor-Couette flow.

That is, we need to focus only on

(70)

\varpi(r)=2A+\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(r),

with
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
given by (
2.3
). In fact,
recalling (
63
) and (
64
) and applying (
70
) we get

(71)

\phi(r)=C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}\log\left(\frac{r}{r_{1}}\right)-A\left(\frac{r^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r}{r_{1}}\right)\right)-\int_{r_{1}}^{r}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\mbox{d}s,

with

(72)

C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)+\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\mbox{d}s\right].

In addition, the operator (
69
) is reduced to the following expression

(73)

\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]\mathsf{g}(r)=\left(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda r\right)\mathsf{g}(r)\\
-\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

5.
Rescaling of the decomposition of the linear operator

Recall that one of the main task of the paper will be to study the kernel and co-kernel of the linear operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda]\equiv\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]\Pi_{n}
over
D_{\varepsilon}
where
\Pi_{n}
is just the projector onto
\cos(n\theta)
.

Here, it is important to emphasize that by definition of
D_{\varepsilon}
and
\varpi^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
(see (
7
) and (
2.3
)) we have the following relation

D_{\varepsilon}=\supp(\varpi^{\prime})\times\mathbb{T}=\supp(\varpi^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\kappa})\times\mathbb{T},

where

(74)

\supp(\varpi^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\kappa})=I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\cup I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}},

with

I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}=(R_{1}-\varepsilon,R_{1}+\varepsilon),\qquad I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}=(R_{2}-\varepsilon,R_{2}+\varepsilon).

Our procedure will be to do an asymptotic analysis of the linear operator in terms of the smallness parameter
\varepsilon
. In order to do that, we will start writing the operator
\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]
in a more tractable manner.
Firstly, we note that we are considering a domain divided into two disjoint pieces. Then, taking (
74
) into account we can just write

\mathsf{g}(r)=\begin{cases}\mathsf{g}_{1}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}.\end{cases}

Using that information via (
73
), we obtain that linear operator
\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]
admits an expression such as

\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(r)=\begin{cases}\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\vskip 7.11317pt\\
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}},\end{cases}

with

(75)

\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(r):=\left(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda r\right)\mathsf{g}_{1}(r)\\
-\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right)\\
+\frac{1}{n}\int_{R_{1}-\varepsilon}^{r}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,\qquad\text{for }r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},

and

(76)

\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(r):=\left(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda r\right)\mathsf{g}_{2}(r)\\
-\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right)\\
+\frac{1}{n}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{R_{2}-\varepsilon}^{r}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right),\qquad\text{for }r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}.

5.1.
Re-scaling over
I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}
and
I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}

Here, we are going to focus on write the previous operators
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda]
and
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda]
given respectively by (
75
) and (
76
) in a more convenient way that will allow us to handle them later.

In first place, we take into account the change of variables

r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}\mapsto z=(r-R_{i})\varepsilon^{-1}\in(-1,1),\qquad\text{for }i=1,2.

Secondly, recalling the definition of the profile function
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
given by (
2.3
) we obtain

\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)=-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-z),\qquad\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)=+\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(+z).

Thirdly, we simplify the notation using the auxiliary functions

(77)

\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{i}(z):=\mathsf{g}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z),

and

(78)

\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z):=(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)\phi^{\prime}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z).

Finally, combining all of the above, multiplying the resulting equation by
(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)
and using notation (
77
) and (
78
) we obtain

(79)

(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)=\left(\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{1}}(z)+\lambda(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)^{2}\right)\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}(z)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(80)

(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)=\left(\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)+\lambda(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)^{2}\right)\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}(z)\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

with
z\in(-1,1).

Remark 5.1
.

It is important to emphasize that after make these change of variables we get a factor
\varepsilon
in front of each integral term. This is an immediate consequence of the fact that
|I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}|=O(\varepsilon).

Remark 5.2
.

Note that each of the above expressions (
79
) and (
80
) are defined on the unit interval.

Remark 5.3
.

We have multiplied (
75
) and (
76
) by
(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)
respectively only for technical reasons. The main motive is that it will be easier to do the asymptotic analysis of the auxiliary function introduced in (
78
) instead of
\phi^{\prime}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)
.

5.2.
The re-scaled linear operator

Finally, for
z\in(-1,1)
we define the auxiliary functional

(81)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}\\
\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(z):

\displaystyle=(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(R_{1}+\varepsilon z),

(82)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}\\
\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(z):

\displaystyle=(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(R_{2}+\varepsilon z),

which are given by the right hand side of (
79
) and (
80
) respectively.

Here, it is important to write explicitly how we can recover the full expression of the linear operator by going backwards. Thus, let
\mathsf{h}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
given by the expansion

\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon},

with

h_{n}(r)=\begin{cases}h_{n}^{R_{1}}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\\
h_{n}^{R_{2}}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}.\end{cases}

Hence, for
(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}=\left(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\cup I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}\right)\times\mathbb{T}
, we finally get

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[\lambda]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda]h_{n}(r)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}h_{n}^{R_{1}}\\
h_{n}^{R_{2}}\end{pmatrix}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\vskip 7.11317pt\\
\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}h_{n}^{R_{1}}\\
h_{n}^{R_{2}}\end{pmatrix}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}},\end{cases}

or equivalently

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[\lambda]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\begin{cases}\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\cos(n\theta)}{r}\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}}\\
\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}}\end{pmatrix}((r-R_{1})\varepsilon^{-1}),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\vskip 7.11317pt\\
\sum_{n\geq 1}\frac{\cos(n\theta)}{r}\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}}\\
\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}}\end{pmatrix}((r-R_{2})\varepsilon^{-1}),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}},\end{cases}

with
\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda],\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]
given respectively by (
81
)-(
82
) and
\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{i}}(z):=h_{n}^{R_{i}}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)
for
z\in(-1,1).

5.3.
Asymptotic analysis of
\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)

The main task of this section will be to get the asymptotic analysis of
\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)
in terms of the parameter
\varepsilon
. In order to do that, we start recalling expression (
71
), which give us that

\phi^{\prime}(r)=\frac{C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}}{r}-A\left(r-\frac{r_{1}^{2}}{r}\right)-\frac{1}{r}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\,\mbox{d}t.

Then, as an inmediate consequence we get

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)

\displaystyle=(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)\phi^{\prime}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)

\displaystyle=C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}-A((R_{i}+\varepsilon z)^{2}-r_{1}^{2})-\int_{r_{1}}^{R_{i}+\varepsilon z}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t,

with (see (
72
))

\displaystyle C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}

\displaystyle=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)+\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\mbox{d}s\right].

Here, for the interested reader, we refer to the Appendix
11.3
for the details of the computations involved to prove that

(83)

\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}+\varepsilon\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{i}}(z)+\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z),

with (see (
243
), (
244
), (
245
), (
246
) and (
247
))

(84)

\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)\right]-A(R_{i}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}),

(85)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{1}}(z)

\displaystyle=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\log(r_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})-\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})\right]-2AR_{1}z,

(86)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(z)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{1}}(0)-2AR_{2}z-\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2},

and

(87)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{1}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}{\log(r_{2}/r_{1})}-Az^{2}-\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(-t)\,\mbox{d}t,

(88)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{1}}(1)+A(1-z^{2})-\left[\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(+t)\,\mbox{d}t-(R_{1}+R_{2})\right],

with
\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
given explicitly by (
242
).

Remark 5.4
.

Here, we emphasize that
\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
is a constant that depends only on
\varepsilon
and
\kappa
.

Remark 5.5
.

Note that
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)=O(1)
for
i=1,2
in terms of the smallness parameters
\varepsilon
and
\kappa
.

6.
One dimensionality of the kernel of the linear operator

The following section consists on two well-differentiated parts. On the one hand, we will prove that there exists an element in the kernel of the linear operator. On the other hand, we will prove that the kernel is exactly equal to the span of this
element. In summary, the main result of this section is to prove the following result:

Theorem 6.1
.

For any
M>1,
there exist positive
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(M)
and positive
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(M)
such that for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
,
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
m<M
, we can find
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in\mathbb{R}
and a
\frac{2\pi}{m}
-periodic and non-identically zero function
h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
solving

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=0,

where the functional
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}[\lambda]
is given in (
68
).
In addition, the kernel
\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
on
X(D_{\varepsilon})
is the span of
h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
and the regularity of the solution is in fact
h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in C^{\infty}(D_{\varepsilon}
).

Remark 6.2
.

Importantly,
h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)
, with
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
and
r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}
, depends non trivially on
\theta
.

Remark 6.3
.

It is important to emphasize that the smallness of
\varepsilon
does not depend on
\kappa
.

Remark 6.4
.

The speed of the rotation
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
satisfies the expansion in terms of
\varepsilon
-parameter:

(89)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=\lambda_{0}+\lambda^{1}_{\kappa,m}\varepsilon+\lambda^{2}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\varepsilon^{2},

where

\lambda_{0}=\frac{u^{\theta}_{\textsf{TC}}(R_{2})}{R_{2}}=\frac{1}{R_{2}}\left(AR_{2}+\frac{B}{R_{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\lambda^{1}_{\kappa,m}=O(1),

and

\displaystyle|\lambda^{2}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}|\leq C(M).

Remark 6.5
.

In the rest of the section we avoid to use subscripts
\varepsilon,\kappa,m
in expression (
89
), which we will ignore in favor of readability. We will write with abuse of notation
\lambda=\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}
. Similarly, in some cases where it is clear from the context, we can simply write
h=h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
with expansion

h(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}.

6.1.
Proof of existence.

In order to show the existence of
(\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m})
solving

\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=0,

we fix
m\in\mathbb{N}
with
1\leq m<M
, and suppose that
h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)=h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r)\cos(m\theta)
consists only on the
m
-th mode of the expansion. Thus, we have reduced our infinite-dimensional problem for every
n\geq 1
to a single problem for
n=m
.
That is, using the notation introduced previously, we take

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
\mathsf{a}(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
\mathsf{b}(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.

where functions
\mathsf{a}
and
\mathsf{b}
depend on
\varepsilon,\kappa
and
m
but we do not make this dependence explicit for sake of simplicity. Thus, our problem translates into finding
(\lambda,\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\in\mathbb{R}\times C^{2+\alpha}(-1,1)\times C^{2+\alpha}(-1,1)
solving

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathsf{a}\\
\mathsf{b}\end{array}\right)=0,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathsf{a}\\
\mathsf{b}\end{array}\right)=0,

where
\mathcal{L}^{R_{i}}_{m,\varepsilon}
are given in (
81
) and (
82
) or equivalently by the right-hand side of (
79
) and (
80
). After that, the solution
(\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},\,h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m})
will be given by

\displaystyle\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=\lambda,

\displaystyle h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)=\mathsf{a}\left(\frac{r-R_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)\cos(m\theta)\quad\text{for $r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}$},

\displaystyle h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)=\mathsf{b}\left(\frac{r-R_{2}}{\varepsilon}\right)\cos(m\theta)\quad\text{for $r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}$}.

In order to solve (
6.1
) and (
6.1
) we introduce the ansatzs

(98)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathsf{a}(z)&=\hskip 35.85031pt\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon^{2},\\
\mathsf{b}(z)&=\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(z)\varepsilon,\end{aligned}\right.

and

(99)

\lambda=\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2},

with

(100)

\lambda_{0}=-\frac{\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{2}}}{R_{2}^{2}}=A\left(1-\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2R_{2}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)}\right)-\frac{\gamma}{R_{2}^{2}\log(r_{2}/r_{1})},

where
\mathsf{a}_{1}
,
\mathsf{b}_{0}
and
\lambda_{1}
will depend on
\kappa
and
m
but they will not depend on
\varepsilon
. In addition, the remaining terms
\mathsf{a}^{\varepsilon}_{2}
,
\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}
and
\lambda^{\varepsilon}_{2}
depend on
\varepsilon
,
\kappa
and
m
.

Remark 6.6
.

Here, it is important to emphasize that the speed
\lambda
of the rotation that we construct is related with the localization of the “legs” of the trapezoid given by the profile
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
and the circulation
\gamma
of the Taylor-Couette flow. More specifically, taking into account (see (
4
)) that

\gamma=(-1)\left(\frac{A}{2}(r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2})+B\log(r_{2}/r_{1})\right),

and applying that into (
100
), we finally arrive to the desired expression

(101)

\lambda_{0}=\frac{u^{\theta}_{\textsf{TC}}(R_{2})}{R_{2}}.

Remark 6.7
.

As a consequence of the above expression we take
R_{2}\in(r_{1},r_{2})
such that
u^{\theta}_{\textsf{TC}}(R_{2})\neq 0
to construct time-periodic solutions with angular velocity of order
O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
.

Remark 6.8
.

It is important to note that in the particular case
u^{\theta}_{\textsf{TC}}(R_{1})=u^{\theta}_{\textsf{TC}}(R_{2})
our procedure give us that at the first order in
\varepsilon
, if
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})
is in the kernel of
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
also
(\mathsf{b},\mathsf{a})
is in the kernel of
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
for the same value of
\lambda
.
For that reason, to avoid technicalities, we will assume that
R_{1},R_{2}\in(r_{1},r_{2})
such that
u^{\theta}_{\textsf{TC}}(R_{1})\neq u^{\theta}_{\textsf{TC}}(R_{2})
.

Putting our ansatz (
98
), (
99
) into (
6.1
), (
6.1
) reduces the problem to solving the following system of equations

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}]\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\hskip 34.14322pt\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon^{2}\\
\hskip-51.21504pt\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(z)\varepsilon\end{array}\right)(z)&=0,\\
\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}]\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\hskip 34.14322pt\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon^{2}\\
\hskip-51.21504pt\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(z)\varepsilon\end{array}\right)(z)&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

6.1.1.
Asymptotic analysis of the linear operator

In order to get a tractable manner to solve the above system, we start introducing the ansatz for the eigenfunction given by (
98
) into (
79
)-(
80
). This implies that

(102)

\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{a}\\
\mathsf{b}\end{pmatrix}(z)=\left[\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{1}}(z)+\lambda(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)^{2}\right]\left(\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon^{2}\right)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\left(\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\varepsilon^{2}\right)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\left(\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)+\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\varepsilon\right)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\left(\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\varepsilon^{2}\right)\,\mbox{d}s=0,

and

(103)

\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{a}\\
\mathsf{b}\end{pmatrix}(z)=\left[\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)+\lambda(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)^{2}\right]\left(\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(z)\varepsilon\right)\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\left(\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\varepsilon^{2}\right)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\left(\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)+\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\varepsilon\right)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\left(\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\varepsilon^{2}\right)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\left(\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)+\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\varepsilon\right)\,\mbox{d}s=0.

The next step will be to introduce also the ansantz for the eigenvalue given by (
99
) into the previous system of equations. But before that, it will be convenient to use (
83
) to write

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)+\lambda(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}+\varepsilon\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{i}}(z)+\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z))+(\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2})(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\lambda_{0}R_{i}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\lambda_{0}2R_{i}z+\lambda_{1}R_{i}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{i}}(z)\right)\varepsilon

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\lambda_{0}z^{2}+\lambda_{1}2zR_{i}+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{i}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}2zR_{i}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}z^{2}\varepsilon^{2}\right)\varepsilon^{2}.

For simplicity, in the rest of this section we will use the notation

(104)

\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)+\lambda(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)^{2}=\alpha_{0}^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{0}]+\alpha_{1}^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\varepsilon+\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\varepsilon^{2},

with

(105)

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{0}]:

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}R_{i}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}},

(106)

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z):

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}2R_{i}z+\lambda_{1}R_{i}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{i}}(z),

(107)

\displaystyle\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z):

\displaystyle=\lambda_{0}z^{2}+\lambda_{1}2zR_{i}+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{i}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}2zR_{i}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}z^{2}\varepsilon^{2}.

At this point, we have all the ingredients to write (
102
) and (
103
) in a much more manageable way. In first place, we note that we can decompose
\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]
as follows

(108)

\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{a}\\
\mathsf{b}\end{pmatrix}=\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}]\varepsilon+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon^{2}+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{3},

with

(109)

\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}](z)=\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

(110)

\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)+\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(111)

\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\left(\mathsf{a_{1}}(z)+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon\right)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Proceeding in a similar manner, we can also decompose
\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]
as follows

(112)

\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{a}\\
\mathsf{b}\end{pmatrix}=\alpha_{0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{2}+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{3},

with

(113)

\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=\alpha_{0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

(114)

\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(115)

\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)=\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

6.1.2.
Dividing the problem into main and lower order terms.

Taking into account these two decompositions, our problem translates into solve the system

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}]\varepsilon+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon^{2}+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{3}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{2}+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{3}

\displaystyle=0.

In fact, since
\lambda_{0}
is defined by (
100
) in such a way that
\alpha_{0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}]=0
we just have to solve

(116)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}]+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{2}&=0,\\
\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\varepsilon^{2}&=0,\end{aligned}\right.

where we have use that
\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]
due to the fact that the term depending of
\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
is multiplied by the factor
\alpha_{0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}]=0
.

In order to start solving (
116
) we need to use the fact that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}](z)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{m,0}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}](z)+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{m,0}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

where (see (
109
), (
113
))

(117)

\mathcal{T}_{m,0}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}](z)=\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)-R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{1}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(118)

\mathcal{Q}_{m,0}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)-R_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Consequently, the use of the asymptotic expansion of the terms
\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1},\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{1}
near
\varepsilon=0
allow us to split the problem of solving the most involved system (
116
) into the study of solving the following two system of equations given by (
119
) and (
120
).

Now, we know explicitly the value of
\lambda_{0}
. Thus, we start solving

(119)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{T}_{m,0}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0};\lambda_{0}]&=0,\\
\mathcal{Q}_{m,0}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{1};\lambda_{0}]&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

After solve the above system, we will have an explicity expression for
\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{0}
. Hence, we have all the necessary ingredients to solve via a fixed point argument the system

(120)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \left(\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{0}]\mbox{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon&=0,\\
\left(\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}]\mbox{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Here, it is important emphasize that we just want to solve system (
120
) for unknowns
\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}
.

Remark 6.9
.

Just before and from now on we will repeatedly use the notation
\mathcal{T}[\mathsf{p};\mathsf{q}]
for an operator
\mathcal{T}
dependent on variable functions
\mathsf{p},\mathsf{q}
where
\mathsf{p}
is an unknown and
\mathsf{q}
is previously known.

6.1.3.
Solving the main order

In this section, we are going to solve the system (
119
).
Equivalently, we just have to solve for
\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0}
and
\lambda_{1}
the following system of equations

(121)

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{1}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s

\displaystyle=0,

(122)

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s

\displaystyle=0,

with

(123)

\mathsf{p}_{i}(m):=R_{i}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{i}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}.

Note that (
121
) implies that
\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)\equiv\mathsf{a}_{1}
is just a constant value given by

(124)

\mathsf{a}_{1}:=\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]}\mathsf{p}_{1}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Recalling the circulation of the Taylor-Couette flow (see (
4
)) and the definition of
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}
given by (
84
) we have that
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}=-(AR_{i}^{2}+B)
. Moreover, taking into account (
105
) and the fact that
B\neq 0
, we get

(125)

\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]=\lambda_{0}R_{1}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{1}}=B\frac{R_{1}^{2}-R_{2}^{2}}{R_{2}^{2}}\neq 0.

Before solve (
122
) we have to pick
\lambda_{1}
appropriately.
Notice that from (
122
) we see that
\mathsf{b}_{0}
must be of the form

(126)

\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)=\frac{C}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)},

for some free constant
C
. Plugging the above expression into (
122
) one finds that

(127)

1=\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s,

which is an equation for
\lambda_{1}
.

Therefore, taking into account all the above, the procedure to solve (
119
) or equivalently (
121
), (
122
) follows the next steps. Firstly, we determine the value of
\lambda_{1}
solving (
127
). Secondly, we determine
\mathsf{b}_{0}
using expression (
126
). Lastly, we obtain the constant value of
\mathsf{a}_{1}
via (
124
).

6.1.4.
Determining the value of
\lambda_{1}

Here, we are going to determine the value of
\lambda_{1}
satisfying (
127
). In order to do that, we observe (see (
106
) and (
86
)) that
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)
is nothing more than a linear function that can be written as

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}z+\lambda_{1}R_{2}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0).

In addition, using (
101
) we note that

(128)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]^{\prime}(z)=(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}=\frac{2B}{R_{2}}.

Therefore, we have that
\mathrm{sgn}(\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]^{\prime}(z))=\mathrm{sgn}(B)
for all
|z|\leq 1
. Consequently, the function
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)
is strictly increasing/decreasing depending only of the sign of parameter
B
.

The task of the next lemma will be to identify the value of
\lambda_{1}
solving (
127
).

Lemma 6.10
.

For any
M>1,
there exist
\kappa^{\prime}_{0}=\kappa^{\prime}_{0}(M)
such that for all
0<\kappa\leq\kappa^{\prime}_{0}
and
1\leq m<M
, we can find
\lambda_{1}
(depending on
\kappa
and
m
) such that (
127
) holds.
In addition, fixed
m
and
\kappa
this solution is unique and satisfies

\lambda_{1}=\lambda^{*}-\beta(M),\qquad 0<\beta(M)\ll 1,

where
\lambda^{*}
is given by (
130
) and
\beta(M)
just depends on
M
(it does not depend on either
\kappa
or
m
).

We start by focusing our attention on the case
B>0
since the details for the other case follow along the same lines. Therefore, we will leave to the interested reader the details for the case
B<0
.

First, it is important to recall that for the case
B>0
we know that
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)
is a strictly increasing linear function. In particular, we have that

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\leq\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)=\frac{2B}{R_{2}}+\lambda_{1}R_{2}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0),

with

\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0)=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\log(r_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})-\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})\right]-\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}.

A priori we do not know what is the sign of
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)
, but we can assure that

(129)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\leq 0,\qquad\text{for all }|z|\leq 1,

taking
\lambda_{1}\in(-\infty,\lambda^{\ast})
, where
\lambda^{\ast}
is defined via the following relation

(130)

\lambda^{\ast}=-\frac{1}{R_{2}^{2}}\left(\frac{2B}{R_{2}}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0)\right).

Finally, we also need to obtain a lower bound of
\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)
for
1\leq m<M
. Recalling the definition of
\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)
thought (
123
) and the fact that
\mathcal{S}_{m}(\cdot):=\sinh(m\log(\cdot))
with
\sinh(\cdot)
strictly increasing function, we obtain

(131)

\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\geq\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{M}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{1}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{1}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{M}(r_{2}/r_{1})}=:\mathfrak{m}(M)>0.

Proof of Lemma
6.10
.

Let me define the auxiliary function

I(\tilde{\lambda}):=\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s.

Applying the fact that
\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\leq 0
(see Lemma
2.6
) and (
129
) we have that
I:(-\infty,\lambda^{\ast})\rightarrow[0,\infty)
is a non-negative function. In addition, it is continuous, strictly increasing with
I^{\prime}(\tilde{\lambda})>0
and satisfying

(132)

\lim_{\tilde{\lambda}\to-\infty}I(\tilde{\lambda})=0.

In fact, we obtain the following lower bound

\displaystyle\frac{I(\tilde{\lambda})}{\mathfrak{m}(M)}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s\geq\int_{-1}^{1-\kappa}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{-1}^{1-\kappa}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)+1/2}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s-\frac{1}{2}\int_{-1}^{1-\kappa}\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{\|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|_{L^{1}([-1,1-\kappa])}}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1-\kappa)|}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2R_{2}(\lambda_{0}-A)}\log\left(\frac{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1-\kappa)|}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](-1)|}\right).

Then, applying again Lemma
2.6
and using (
128
) we have

\frac{I(\tilde{\lambda})}{\mathfrak{m}(M)}\geq-\frac{C\kappa}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1-\kappa)|}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{R_{2}}{2B}\log\left(\frac{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1-\kappa)|}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](-1)|}\right).

As an intermediate step, we study each of the above terms separately. In first place, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1-\kappa)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1)-\kappa(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1)-\kappa\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\leq-\kappa\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\ ,

where we have used (
128
). Moreover, we have seen that
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\leq 0
for all
|z|\leq 1
thanks to (
129
). This implies that

(133)

-\frac{\kappa}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1-\kappa)|}\geq-\frac{R_{2}}{2B}.

For the other term, we can ensure that for any
0<\beta\ll 1
there exist
\kappa_{0}^{\prime}(\beta)
small enough such that

(134)

0<\frac{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](1-\kappa)|}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\tilde{\lambda}](-1)|}<\beta,

for all
\tilde{\lambda}
satisfying
0<\lambda^{\ast}-\tilde{\lambda}<\kappa_{0}^{\prime}
with
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}^{\prime}
.

Now, we have all the ingredients to conclude. We just need to combine (
133
) and (
134
) to get

\frac{I(\tilde{\lambda})}{\mathfrak{m}(M)}\geq-\frac{R_{2}}{2B}\left(C+\frac{1}{2}\log(\beta)\right).

Then, taking
\beta
small enough such that

(135)

-\frac{R_{2}}{2B}\left(C+\frac{1}{2}\log(\beta)\right)>\frac{1}{\mathfrak{m}(M)},

we have proved that
I(\tilde{\lambda})>1
for all
\tilde{\lambda}
satisfying
0<\lambda^{\ast}-\tilde{\lambda}<\kappa_{0}^{\prime}(\beta)
with
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}^{\prime}(\beta)
and
1\leq m<M
. Here, it is important to emphasize that
\beta
given by (
135
) only depends on
M
.

Combining the lower bound with (
132
) and the fact that
I(\tilde{\lambda})
is strictly increasing, we
conclude that there exists a unique
\lambda_{1}=\lambda^{\ast}-\beta(M)
solving (
127
) for all
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}^{\prime}
and
1\leq m<M
.
∎

After choose a suitable
\lambda_{1}<\lambda^{\ast}
by Lemma
6.10
, we obtain
\mathsf{b}_{0}
through equation (
126
). Now,
we have all the ingredients to prove the following result.

Lemma 6.11
.

For any
M>1,
there exists
\kappa_{0}=\kappa_{0}(M)
such that for
1\leq m<M
and
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
, we can find a solution
(\lambda_{1},\,\mathsf{b}_{0})\in\mathbb{R}\times C^{\infty}([-1,1])
of equation (
122
). In addition, fixed
m
and
\kappa
, this solution is unique (modulo multiplication by constant) and satisfies

(136)

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)},

(137)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2B}{R_{2}^{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}\right]-1}\right)-\frac{\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0)}{R_{2}^{2}}+\mathsf{error},

where

\displaystyle|\mathsf{error}|\leq C(M)\kappa.

Moreover, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}\leq C(M),

\displaystyle\|\partial_{z}^{k)}\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,\,1])}\leq C(k,M),\quad k=0,1,2,...

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}_{1}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]}\frac{R_{1}\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{1}/r_{1})}{R_{2}\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{2})}.

Remark 6.12
.

It is important to emphasize that constant
C(M)
depend on
M
but it does not depend on neither
m
or
\kappa
, for
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
1\leq m<M
.

Proof.

We start the proof by explaining that, as before, we are considering only the case
B>0
. The remaining case, i.e.
B<0
follows my straightforward modifications.

In first place, we note that

\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)},

solves (
122
) with linear function
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)
given by

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}z+\lambda_{1}R_{2}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0).

Since
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)<0
for all
|z|\leq 1
we obtain that its inverse, that is,
\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)
is a
C^{\infty}
-function on the unit interval
[-1,1]
. In addition, since
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda^{\ast}](1)=0
by definition of
\lambda^{\ast}
, we have that
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0)=-\lambda_{\ast}R_{2}^{2}-(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}
and consequently we can just write

(138)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}(z-1)+(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{\ast})R_{2}^{2}.

Thus, a direct calculation yields

\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}^{2}=\frac{1}{|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{\ast}|R_{2}^{2}}.

Recalling that
\lambda_{1}
given by Lemma
6.10
satisfies
\lambda_{\ast}-\lambda_{1}=\beta(M)
we get that
\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq C(M)
. Proceeding in a similar way, we obtain the same type of bounds for its derivatives, i.e.

\|\partial_{z}^{k)}\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,\,1])}\leq C(k,M).

From (
124
) and (
127
) we have that

\mathsf{a}_{1}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]}\mathsf{p}_{1}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]}\frac{\mathsf{p}_{1}(m)}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]}\frac{R_{1}\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{1}/r_{1})}{R_{2}\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{2})}.

Finally, in order to get (
137
) we proceed as follow. From the identity (
127
) we get

(139)

1=\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)-1/2}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)|}\,\mbox{d}s+\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}{2}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)|}\,\mbox{d}s\\
=\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}{2}\left(\Lambda_{1}+\frac{1}{(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}}\log\left(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](+1)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](-1)}\right)\right),

where

\Lambda_{1}:=2\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)-1/2}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)|}\,\mbox{d}s.

Solving (
139
) we get

\lambda_{1}R_{2}^{2}+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0)=(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}\left(1+\frac{2}{\exp\left[(\lambda_{0}-A)2R_{2}\left(\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}-\Lambda_{1}\right)\right]-1}\right),

and using (
128
) we finally arrive to

\lambda_{1}=\frac{2B}{R_{2}^{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}-\Lambda_{1}\right)\right]-1}\right)-\frac{\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0)}{R_{2}^{2}}.

Consequently

\left|\lambda_{1}-\frac{2B}{R_{2}^{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}\right]-1}\right)+\frac{\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(0)}{R_{2}^{2}}\right|\\
=\frac{2B}{R_{2}^{3}}\frac{2\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}\right]\left(1-\exp\left[-\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\Lambda_{1}\right]\right)}{\left(\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}-\Lambda_{1}\right)\right]-1\right)\left(\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}\right]-1\right)}=:\mathsf{error}.

Now, we can estimate each of the terms of the last factor that involve the term
\Lambda_{1}
. Note that the term appearing in the numerator can be bounded by

\left|1-\exp\left[-\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\Lambda_{1}\right]\right|\leq\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}|\Lambda_{1}|\right]|\Lambda_{1}|.

In addition, using the fact that
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{\ast}](1)=0
we get

|\Lambda_{1}|\leq 2\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)+1/2|}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)|}\,\mbox{d}s\leq\frac{C\kappa}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)|}=\frac{C\kappa}{(\lambda_{\ast}-\lambda_{1})R_{2}^{2}}

where
C
is a universal constant coming from Lemma
2.6
. Proceeding as before, since
\lambda_{1}
given by Lemma
6.10
satisfies
0<\lambda_{\ast}-\lambda_{1}=\beta(M)
we have proved that

|\Lambda_{1}|\leq\frac{C\kappa}{\beta(M)R_{2}^{2}}.

For the denominator it is sufficient to note that

\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\leq R_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{M}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{M}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{1}(r_{2}/r_{1})}=:\mathfrak{M}(M),\qquad\text{for all}\quad 1\leq m<M,

and

\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}-\Lambda_{1}\right)\right]-1\\
\geq\exp\left[\frac{2B}{R_{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\mathfrak{M}(M)}-\frac{C\kappa}{\beta(M)R_{2}^{2}}\right)\right]-1>C(M),\qquad\text{if}\quad\frac{2}{\mathfrak{M}(M)}>\frac{C\kappa}{\beta(M)R_{2}^{2}}.

Then, taking
\kappa<\kappa_{0}\equiv\min\{\kappa_{0}^{\prime},\kappa_{0}^{\prime\prime}\}
, where
\kappa_{0}^{\prime}
is given by Lemma
6.10
and
\kappa_{0}^{\prime\prime}=\frac{2\beta(M)R_{2}^{2}}{C\,\mathfrak{M}(M)}
, we have proved that
|\mathsf{error}|\leq C(M)\kappa
for all
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
1\leq m<M
.
∎

6.1.5.
Solving the lower order terms via a fixed point argument

The main task of this section is to solve (
120
) for the unknowns
\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}
. We rewrite it here for convenience.

\left\{\begin{aligned} \left(\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{0}]\mbox{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon&=0,\\
\left(\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}]\mbox{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

We will proceed as before by considering the Taylor expansion of
\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}
around
\varepsilon=0
.
Before that, we need to take care of the fact that term
\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
, which appears on
\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]
through the term
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)
, satisfies that
\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}=O(1)
but
\left(\partial_{\varepsilon}\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}\right)(z)=O(\varepsilon^{-1})
in terms of
\varepsilon
.

With a little abuse of notation, in the rest of this section, we redefine term
\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)
as (see (
107
))

(140)

\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z):=\lambda_{0}z^{2}+\lambda_{1}2zR_{2}+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{2}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}2zR_{2}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}z^{2}\varepsilon^{2},

where the term
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)
will be study separately.

After this, the main task of this section is to solve the coupled system

\left(\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{0}]\mbox{d}\delta\right)+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\varepsilon=0

together with

\left(\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\right)\\
+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\varepsilon=0.

Now, we can use Taylor’s expansion to obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{T}_{m,0}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1}]\mbox{d}\delta,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathcal{Q}_{m,0}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0}]\mbox{d}\delta,

where (see (
110
), (
114
))

(141)

\mathcal{T}_{m,0}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]=\alpha_{1}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)+\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\\
+R_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{1}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{1}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(142)

\mathcal{Q}_{m,0}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{2,0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
+R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{1})-\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/R_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-R_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Remark 6.13
.

Here, it is important to emphasize that term
\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]
is defined via (
140
).

In fact, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{m,0}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0}]+\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}],

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{m,0}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]+\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1}]

with

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0}]

\displaystyle:=\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)-R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{1}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]

\displaystyle:=\alpha_{1}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)+R_{1}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{1}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(143)

\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]:=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{2,0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
-R_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1}]:=R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{1})-\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/R_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Remark 6.14
.

From now on we will repeatedly use the decomposition
\mathcal{T}[\mathsf{p};\mathsf{q}]=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}[\mathsf{p};\mathsf{q}]+\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}[\mathsf{q}]
to emphasize that unknown appear only on the term
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}[\mathsf{p};\mathsf{q}]
and the other is fully determined.

Taking into account all the above considerations, the system (
120
) can be written as

\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0}](z)+\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\right)\\
+\varepsilon\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1}](z)\mbox{d}\delta+\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},](z)\right)=0,

and

\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1}](z)+\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\right)\\
+\varepsilon\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta+\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\right)=0.

Here, it is important to note that each of the terms in parentheses is of order
O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
.
Consequently, we finally get for
\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}
the system

(144)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0}](z)

\displaystyle=A_{0}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\varepsilon A_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{0}](z),

(145)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)

\displaystyle=B_{0}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\varepsilon B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z),

where

\displaystyle A_{0}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z):

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}\right)[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

\displaystyle B_{0}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z):

\displaystyle=-\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1}](z)-\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

and

A_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z):=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1}](z)\mbox{d}\delta-\mathcal{T}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z),

B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z):=-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta-\mathcal{Q}_{m,\varepsilon}^{3}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z).

Here, we note that Lemma
6.11
together with (
248
) yields

\displaystyle\|A_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M),

\displaystyle\|B_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M).

To handle
A_{1}
and
B_{1}
we have to make an intermediate step. Applying Lemma
6.10
into definition (
111
) and (
115
) we get

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}^{3}_{m,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C(M)\left(\|\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|+|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\|\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{Q}^{3}_{m,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\leq

\displaystyle C(M)\left(|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

Combining the above with Lemma
6.11
together with (
249
) and (
250
) we obtain

\displaystyle\|A_{1}\|_{L^{2}}
