\displaystyle\leq C(M)\left(\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|+|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\|\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\|B_{1}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\left(|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|+\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

6.1.6.
Inverting the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}

In the following two lemmas we are going to learn how to invert the left hand side of (
145
). In order to do that, we write explicitly (
143
), that is the left hand side of (
145
):

\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]:=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{2,0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

where in the last term we have used (
123
).

In order to invert the above operator we are going to split the argument in two parts. Firstly, we are going to start inverting a simplified expression as follows. We omit their proof because it follows by direct computation.

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
\lambda_{0}
given by expression (
100
),
\lambda_{1}
be as in Lemma
6.10
and let
\mathsf{F}\in L^{2}([-1,1])
such that
\int_{-1}^{1}\mathsf{F}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0
. Then

\mathsf{g}(z):=\mathsf{F}(z)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)=\frac{\mathsf{F}(z)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)},

solves

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{g}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{g}(s)\emph{d}s=\mathsf{F}(z).

Here, we recall expression (
140
) that allows us to write

\displaystyle\alpha_{2,0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=\lambda_{0}z^{2}+\lambda_{1}2zR_{2}+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{2}^{2}=:\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{2}^{2}+\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z).

Now, we have all the ingredients to invert
\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}
.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}
be as in Lemma
6.15
and let
\mathsf{G}\in L^{2}([-1,1])
. Then, taking

\mu:=\frac{1}{R_{2}^{2}}\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}\left(\mathsf{G}(s)-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s},

the function

\mathsf{g}(z):=\left(\mathsf{G}(z)-\mu R_{2}^{2}\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)=\frac{\mathsf{G}(z)-\mu R_{2}^{2}\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)},

solves

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{g}(z)+\left(\mu R_{2}^{2}+\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{g}(s)\emph{d}s=\mathsf{G}(z).

That is, we solve

(146)

\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{g},\mu;\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=\mathsf{G}(z),

and we have the bound

(147)

|\mu|\leq C(M)\|\mathsf{G}-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}.

Proof.

It is immediate to verify that
\mathsf{F}(z):=\mathsf{G}(z)-\left(\mu R_{2}^{2}+\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)
satisfies that

\int_{-1}^{1}F(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0

by the definition of
\mu
. Then, we can apply directly Lemma
6.15
to obtain (
146
).

To conclude (
147
), on the one hand, we note that

\displaystyle\left|\int_{-1}^{1}\left(\mathsf{G}(s)-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathsf{G}-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}

\displaystyle\leq C(M)\|\mathsf{G}-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])},

where in the last step we have used Lemmas
6.11
and
2.6
.

On the other hand, since
\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)=\left(\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\right)^{-1}
and
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\leq\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)<0
we have

\left|\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right|\geq\frac{1}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)|}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s=\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}{|\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)|},

where in the last step we have used (
127
). In fact, applying the lower bound (
131
) and the fact that
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)=(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{\ast})R_{2}^{2}
with
\lambda_{1}
given by Lemma
6.10
we arrive to

\left|\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right|\geq\frac{\mathfrak{m}(M)}{\beta(M)R_{2}^{2}}.

Combining both we have

|\mu|\leq\frac{1}{R_{2}^{2}}\frac{\beta(M)R_{2}^{2}}{\mathfrak{m}(M)}C(M)\|\mathsf{G}-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq C(M)\|\mathsf{G}-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}.

∎

6.1.7.
The fixed point argument

At this point we have all the required ingredients to solve the system (
144
), (
145
) for
\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}
and
\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}
. We rewrite the system here for convenience:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\lambda_{0}](z)

\displaystyle=A_{0}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\varepsilon A_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{0}](z),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)

\displaystyle=B_{0}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)+\varepsilon B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z).

With a little abuse of notation, since
\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}
are already determined at this point of the proof, we just write the system in terms of the unknowns
\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}
. That is,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}](z)

\displaystyle=A_{0}(z)+\varepsilon A_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)

\displaystyle=B_{0}(z)+\varepsilon B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z).

Now, inverting
\widehat{\mathcal{Q}}_{m}^{2}
via Lemma
6.16
we get

(148)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}=\frac{1}{R_{2}^{2}}\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}\left(B_{0}(s)+\varepsilon B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](s)-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s},

(149)

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)=\left(B_{0}(z)+\varepsilon B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)-\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{2}^{2}\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(z),

and rewriting the equation for
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{m}^{2}
in an explicit way, we have

(150)

\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)-R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/R_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{2})}{m\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/r_{2})}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=A_{0}(z)+\varepsilon A_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z).

Then, solving (
148
), (
149
), (
150
) will give us a solution of our original system (
144
), (
145
).

Now, to obtain a solution of (
148
), (
149
) and (
150
) we firstly introduce (
148
) and (
149
) in the second term of the left hand side of (
150
) and (
148
) in the right hand side of (
149
). In this way, a solution of

(151)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{R_{2}^{2}}\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}\left(B_{0}(s)+\varepsilon B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](s)-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s},

(152)

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{B}_{0}(z)+\varepsilon\widetilde{B}_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z),

(153)

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{A}_{0}(z)+\varepsilon\widetilde{A}_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z),

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{0}(z)

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\left(B_{0}(z)-\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}\left(B_{0}(s)-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\left(B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)-\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}\right),

and

\widetilde{A}_{0}(z):=A_{0}(z)+R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/R_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{2})}{m\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/r_{2})}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)B_{0}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\left(\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}\left(B_{0}(s)-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}\right)R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/R_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{2})}{m\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/r_{2})}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}s,

\widetilde{A}_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z):=A_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)+R_{1}R_{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/R_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{2})}{m\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{1}/r_{2})}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\left(\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}B_{1}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}\right)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}s,

will give us a solution of (
148
), (
149
) and (
150
).

In order to solve (
151
), (
152
) and (
153
) we can use a contraction argument on parameter
\varepsilon
. Indeed, let us call
F_{\lambda}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)
,
F_{B}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)
and
F_{A}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)
to the right hand side of (
151
), (
152
) and (
153
) respectively. We define the constants values

C_{\lambda}:=\left|\frac{1}{R_{2}^{2}}\frac{\int_{-1}^{1}\left(B_{0}(s)-\beta[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)\right)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}{\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)|^{2}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\,\mbox{d}s}\right|,

and

C_{B}:=\|\tilde{B}_{0}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])},\quad C_{A}:=\|\tilde{A}_{0}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}.

Remark 6.17
.

Let us emphasize that
C_{A}
,
C_{B}
and
C_{\lambda}
are uniformly bounded by some
C(M).

Note that if
|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|<2C_{\lambda}
,
\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{B}
and
\|\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A}
, taking
\varepsilon
small enough (with respect to a constant that just depends on
M
) we have

\displaystyle|F_{\lambda}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]|<2C_{\lambda},

\displaystyle\|F_{B}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{B},

\displaystyle\|F_{A}[\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A}.

In addition, for any pair

u=(\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}),\qquad\tilde{u}=(\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\tilde{\mathsf{a}}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\mathsf{b}}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\lambda}_{2}^{\varepsilon})

satisfying
|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\lambda}^{\varepsilon}_{2}|<2C_{\lambda}
,
\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\tilde{\mathsf{b}}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{B}
and
\|\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\tilde{\mathsf{a}}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A}
we have that

\displaystyle|F_{\lambda}[u]-F_{\lambda}[\tilde{u}]|\leq\varepsilon C(M)\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\|F_{A}[u]-F_{A}[\tilde{u}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq\varepsilon C(M)\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}},

\displaystyle\|F_{B}[u]-F_{B}[\tilde{u}]\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq\varepsilon C(M)\|u-\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}}.

Taking
\varepsilon_{0}:=C(M)^{-1},
we have that right hand side of (
148
), (
149
), (
150
) is a contraction mapping for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}(M).
This implies that there exist
(\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon})\in L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times\mathbb{R}
, with
\|\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<2C_{A}
,
\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}<{2C_{B}}
and
|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|<2C_{\lambda}
solving (
151
), (
152
) and (
153
) for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}(M)
.

Therefore, we have shown the existence of a solution
(\lambda,\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\in\mathbb{R}\times L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
solving (
6.1
) and (
6.1
), with

(154)

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}(z)=

\displaystyle\mathsf{a}_{1}\varepsilon+\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon^{2},

(155)

\displaystyle\mathsf{b}(z)=

\displaystyle\,\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)\varepsilon,

(156)

\displaystyle\lambda=

\displaystyle\,\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2},

with
\lambda_{0}
given by (
100
) and
\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{1}
given by Lemma
6.11

and satisfying

(157)

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq C(M),\quad\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}\leq C(M),\quad|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\leq C(M).

Next we shall show that this solution is smooth.

6.2.
Regularity

To determine the exact regularity of the constructed solution
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})
we proceed as follows. First, we recall that the system (
6.1
), (
6.1
) can be written using (
79
), (
80
) as

(158)

\frac{\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)}{R_{1}+\varepsilon z}\mathsf{a}(z)=\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

(159)

\frac{\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)}{R_{2}+\varepsilon z}\mathsf{b}(z)=\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{a}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

with

(160)

\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}(z):=\lambda_{m}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)^{2}+\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z),\qquad\text{for }i=1,2.

Note that neither
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)
nor
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)
have zeros on the unit interval. More specifically, for the computed eigenvalue
\lambda=\lambda_{0}+\lambda_{1}\varepsilon+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{2}
, we have (see (
104
)) that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]+\alpha_{1}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\varepsilon+\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\varepsilon^{2},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)

\displaystyle=\phantom{\alpha_{0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}]+}\,\,\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\varepsilon+\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\varepsilon^{2}.

On the one hand, recalling (
125
) we have seen that

\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]=B\frac{R_{1}^{2}-R_{2}^{2}}{R_{2}^{2}}\neq 0.

On the other hand, using (
138
) and Lemma
6.10
we have

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\leq\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](1)=-\beta(M),\qquad\text{with }\quad\beta(M)>0.

Consequently, we have

\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)=\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]+O(\varepsilon),\qquad\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)=-\beta(M)\varepsilon+O(\varepsilon^{2}).

Taking
\varepsilon
small enough these two quantities are not zero and we are allow to divide both sides of (
158
), (
159
) by these non-zero factors. In addition, most of the terms that appear on the right side of (
158
), (
159
) are directly
C^{\infty}([-1,1])
-functions since they involved only
\mathcal{S}_{n}(x)=\sinh(n\log(x))
with
x>1
.

Finally, for the rest of the integral terms that appear as

\mathsf{f}(z)=\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{i}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}((-1)^{i}s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{i}+\varepsilon z}{R_{i}+\varepsilon s}\right)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

we are going to use the fact that
\mathsf{f}\in H^{2}([-1,1])
for
\mathsf{g}\in L^{2}([-1,1])
. The result follows directly by taking two derivatives on
\mathsf{f}
to get that

\mathsf{f}^{\prime}(z)=\frac{\varepsilon m}{R_{i}+\varepsilon z}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{i}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}((-1)^{i}s)\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{i}+\varepsilon z}{R_{i}+\varepsilon s}\right)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

\mathsf{f}^{\prime\prime}(z)=\varepsilon m\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}((-1)^{i}z)\mathsf{g}(z)+\left(\frac{\varepsilon m}{R_{i}+\varepsilon z}\right)^{2}\mathsf{f}(z)\\
-\frac{\varepsilon^{2}m}{(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)^{2}}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{i}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}((-1)^{i}s)\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{i}+\varepsilon z}{R_{i}+\varepsilon s}\right)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Therefore, if
\mathsf{g}\in H^{k}([-1,1])
we have that
\mathsf{f}\in H^{k+2}([-1,1])
.

All this yields that if
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\in H^{k}([-1,1])\times H^{k}([-1,1])
then
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\in H^{k+2}([-1,1])\times H^{k+2}([-1,1])
for all
k\geq 0
. Then, we can conclude by a recursive argument that
(\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\in C^{\infty}([-1,1])
.

6.3.
Uniqueness

So far we have shown that there exist
\lambda
and
(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}},\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}})
given by

\displaystyle\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
\mathsf{a}(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
\mathsf{b}(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.

with
(\lambda,\mathsf{a},\mathsf{b})\in\mathbb{R}\times C^{\infty}([-1,1])\times C^{\infty}([-1,1])
solving (
81
), (
82
) for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
and satisfying (
154
), (
155
), (
156
) and (
157
).

To finish the proof of Theorem
6.1
we need to check that the kernel of
\mathcal{L}[\lambda]
, fixed that
\lambda
, is one dimensional. Therefore we have to prove that the solution of system (
81
), (
82
), given by (
6.1
), (
6.1
), (
154
), (
155
), for
\lambda
given by (
156
), is unique modulo multiplication by a constant.
In order to prove our goal, we will distinguish between two cases:

6.3.1.
Case
n=m

Let
(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})\in L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
be a solution of (
6.1
), (
6.1
), with
\lambda
given by (
156
).
Note that
(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})
depends on
\varepsilon
although we do not make explicit this dependence.
Then, let us see that for some constant C we have

\begin{cases}\mathsf{u}(z)=C\mathsf{a}(z),\\
\mathsf{v}(z)=C\mathsf{b}(z).\end{cases}

The system (
6.1
), (
6.1
) is linear in
(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})
and we can assume without loss of generality that

\|\mathsf{u}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}^{2}+\|\mathsf{v}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}^{2}=1.

If it is not the case we only need to normalized the solution and enter that value into the final constant
C
. Now, using (
6.1
) and (
102
), we have that

0=\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{u}\\
\mathsf{v}\end{pmatrix}(z)=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)\mathsf{u}(z)+O(\varepsilon),

but since we have seen that
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)=O(1)
we get that
\mathsf{u}(z)=\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{1}(z)
with
\|\mathsf{u}_{1}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(1)

in terms of
\varepsilon
. This information yields from (
6.1
) and (
103
) that

0=\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{u}\\
\mathsf{v}\end{pmatrix}(z)=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)\mathsf{v}(z)+O(\varepsilon^{2})\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Since
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\varepsilon+\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\varepsilon^{2}
, we divide the above expression by
\varepsilon
to get

(161)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{v}(z)+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=O(\varepsilon).

Now, we introduce the auxiliary functional

(162)

R_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{v}](z):=-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

which can be written as

R_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{v}](z)=R_{0}[\mathsf{v}](z)+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}R_{\delta})[\mathsf{v}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

with

R_{0}[\mathsf{v}](z):=-R_{2}^{2}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(R_{2}/r_{1})\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/R_{2})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Therefore, using (
123
) we have that (
161
) can be written as

(163)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{v}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=O(\varepsilon)-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}R_{\delta})[\mathsf{v}](z)\mbox{d}\delta=:F(z),

where
\|F\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(\varepsilon)
, see (
251
) for details. Just dividing (
163
) by
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)
we have

\mathsf{v}(z)=\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\frac{F(z)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)}.

Thus, recalling the precise form of
\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)
, given by (
136
) we obtain that

(164)

\mathsf{v}(z)=C\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}(z),

where
\|\mathsf{v}_{1}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
and
C
is a constant.

Taking into account all this information and looking again (
6.1
) and (
102
), we obtain

0=\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{1}\\
C\mathsf{b}_{0}+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}\end{pmatrix}(z)=\varepsilon\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)\mathsf{u}_{1}(z)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}(s))\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0.

Then, using (
123
) we have that

\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{u}_{1}(z)-C\mathsf{p}_{1}(m)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=G(z),

where
\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(\varepsilon)
. Recalling the precise form of
\mathsf{a}_{1}
, see (
124
), forces to

(165)

\mathsf{u}_{1}(z)=C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2}(z),

with
\|\mathsf{u}_{2}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
and the same constant
C
that in (
164
).

Finally, we get a coupled system for
(\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{v}_{1})
which is exactly the same as (
149
), (
150
) for
(\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon})
up to the multiplicative constant
C
. More specifically, we have

0=\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon(C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2})\\
C\mathsf{b}_{0}+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}\end{pmatrix}(z)=\varepsilon\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)(C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2}(z))\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2}(s))\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}(s))\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2}(s))\,\mbox{d}s=0,

and

0=\mathcal{L}_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda]\begin{pmatrix}\varepsilon(C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2})\\
C\mathsf{b}_{0}+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}\end{pmatrix}(z)=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)(C\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}(z))\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2}(s))\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}(s))\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{a}_{1}+\varepsilon\mathsf{u}_{2}(s))\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))(C\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)+\varepsilon\mathsf{v}_{1}(s))\,\mbox{d}s=0.

Proceeding as before, we finally arrive to

\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{u}_{2}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{1}(m)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{v}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=CA_{0}(z)+\varepsilon A_{1}[\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{v}_{1};C\mathsf{a}_{1},C\mathsf{b}_{0}],

\mathsf{v}_{1}(z)=\left(CB_{0}(z)+\varepsilon B_{1}[\mathsf{u}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{1};C\mathsf{b}_{0},C\mathsf{a}_{1}](z)-\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{2}^{2}C\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\right)C\mathsf{b}_{0}(z).

This system is linear in
(\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{v}1)
(now
\lambda
is fixed). One can check that
C(\mathsf{a}_{\varepsilon}^{2},\mathsf{b}_{\varepsilon}^{1})
is a solution and by a fixed point argument must be unique. Then
(\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{v}_{1})=C(\mathsf{a}_{\varepsilon}^{2},\mathsf{b}_{\varepsilon}^{1})
(with the same constant
C
as in (
165
) and (
164
)) and consequently we have proved our goal.

6.3.2.
Case
n\neq m

Since
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)=O(1)
and
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)=O(\varepsilon)
we can just write

(166)

\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)\mathsf{u}(z)+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0,

and (dividing the full expression by
\varepsilon
)

(167)

\frac{\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{v}(z)+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0.

Hence, we can divide (
166
) by
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)
to get

(168)

\mathsf{u}(z)=\varepsilon U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z),

where

U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z):=\frac{R_{1}+\varepsilon z}{n\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{R_{1}+\varepsilon z}{n\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+\frac{R_{1}+\varepsilon z}{n\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

The next step will be to introduce (
168
) into the equation (
167
). Hence, we have

(169)

\frac{\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{v}(z)-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
=(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Now, we introduce the auxiliary functional

(170)

W_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{v}](z):=-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

which can be written as

W_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{v}](z)=W_{0}[\mathsf{v}](z)+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}W_{\delta})[\mathsf{v}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

with (using (
123
))

W_{0}[\mathsf{v}](z)=-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Then, using (
170
) we have that (
169
) can be written as

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{v}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=-\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\mathsf{v}(z)\varepsilon-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}W_{\delta})[\mathsf{v}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Introducing a new auxiliary functional
\varepsilon V[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z)
for the right hand side of the above expression, we arrive to

(171)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{v}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=\varepsilon V[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z),

with

V[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z):=-\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\mathsf{v}(z)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}W_{\delta})[\mathsf{v}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](s)\,\mbox{d}s.

In the following lemma we are going to learn how to invert the left hand side of (
171
).

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
\mathsf{G}\in L^{2}([-1,1])
. Then, the unique solution for

(172)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{g}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=\mathsf{G}(z),

is given by

(173)

\mathsf{g}(z)=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)}\left(\mathsf{G}(z)+\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\frac{\mathsf{G}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s\right)

Before their proof, we need to note that the following equivalence holds:

(174)

\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)=\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad n=m.

Proof of (
174
).

One of the implication follows trivially. In order to get the other one, we just need to prove that
\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)
is strictly decreasing for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Since
r_{1}<R_{1}<R_{2}<r_{2}
, taking
\mathsf{A}:=R_{2}/r_{1}
and
\mathsf{B}:=r_{2}/R_{2}
, we can write

\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)=\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{A})\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{B})}{n\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{A}\cdot\mathsf{B})},\qquad\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}>1.

By calculating its derivative, we obtain

\mathcal{S}_{n}^{2}(\mathsf{A}\cdot\mathsf{B})\frac{n}{R_{2}^{2}}\mathsf{p}_{2}^{\prime}(n)=\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathsf{A})\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{B})\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{A}\cdot\mathsf{B})\log(\mathsf{A})+\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathsf{B})\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{A})\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{A}\cdot\mathsf{B})\log(\mathsf{B})\\
-\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{A})\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{B})\mathcal{C}_{n}(\mathsf{A}\cdot\mathsf{B})\log(\mathsf{A}\cdot\mathsf{B})-\frac{1}{n}\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{A})\mathcal{S}_{n}(\mathsf{B}).

Maximizing the function of the right-hand side of the previous expression over the region
\mathsf{A,B}\geq 1
we obtain that its absolute maximum is zero and is reached on the boundary, i.e.
\mathsf{A}=1
or
\mathsf{B}=1
.
Since the above is independent of the parameter
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we get
\mathsf{p}_{2}^{\prime}(n)<0
and conclude our goal.
∎

Now, we have all the ingredients to prove the previous lemma.

Proof of Lemma
6.18
.

If
\mathsf{g}(z)
is a solution of (
172
) then

(175)

\mathsf{g}(z)=\frac{C}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)}+\frac{\mathsf{G}(z)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)},

for some constant
C
. Plugging this expression of
\mathsf{g}
into (
172
) yields

C\left(1-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s\right)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\frac{\mathsf{G}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s=0.

Then, using (
127
) we get

C\left(1-\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}\right)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\frac{\mathsf{G}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s=0,

or equivalently

(176)

C=\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\frac{\mathsf{G}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s,

where in the last step we have used (
174
) to ensure that
\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\neq 0
.

Conversely, it is easy to check that
\mathsf{g}
in (
175
), with
C
given by (
176
), solves the equation (
172
).
∎

To finish, let us introduce the linear functional

I[\mathsf{G}](z):=\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)}\left(\mathsf{G}(z)+\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)}{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)}\int_{-1}^{1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\frac{\mathsf{G}(s)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](s)}\,\mbox{d}s\right).

By using Lemma
6.18
, we have that solution of (
171
) is given by

(177)

\mathsf{v}(z)=\varepsilon I[V[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}]](z).

Remark 6.19
.

To apply Lemma
6.18
and arrive at the above expression (
177
) we must first check that the right-hand side of (
171
) belongs to
L^{2}([-1,1])
, which follows immediately from (
251
).

The coupled system given by (
168
) and (
177
) is a linear contraction on
L^{2}([-1,1])
for
\varepsilon
small
enough. Hence, there exists a unique
(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})\in L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
solving (
168
), (
177
):

\displaystyle\mathsf{u}(z)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon U[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z),

\displaystyle\mathsf{v}(z)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon I[V[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}]](z).

Therefore, the trivial solution
(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})=(0,0)
is the unique solution of (
166
) and (
167
).
Consequently, we have achieved all the conclusions of Theorem
6.1
.

7.
Co-dimension of the image of the linear operator

Let
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
given by Theorem
6.1
. In order to prove that the co-dimension in
Y(D_{\varepsilon})
of the linear operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\equiv D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]
over
X(D_{\varepsilon})
is one-dimensional, we will borrow the argument used for example in
[
45
]
, see also
[
40
,
42
,
43
,
44
]
. Indeed, we will prove that
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
is a Fredholm operator of zero index. Since

\text{dim}(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]))=\text{dim}(Y(D_{\varepsilon})/\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])),

for this kind of operators, we will find that

(178)

\text{dim}(Y(D_{\varepsilon})/\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]))=1,

due to Theorem
6.1
, where we have proved that kernel
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])
of the linearized operator is one-dimensional. Hence, we have seen the third condition of the Crandall-Rabinowitz Theorem.

7.1.
Isomorphism plus compact operator

The main task of this section is to write the linear operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
as an isomorphism plus a compact operator.
In order to prove this, it will be convenient to recall that linear operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
can be decomposed as

\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]h_{n}(r),\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon},

or equivalently

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\begin{cases}\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\begin{pmatrix}h_{n}^{R_{1}}\\
h_{n}^{R_{2}}\end{pmatrix}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\vskip 7.11317pt\\
\sum_{n\geq 1}\cos(n\theta)\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\begin{pmatrix}h_{n}^{R_{1}}\\
h_{n}^{R_{2}}\end{pmatrix}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}},\end{cases}

with

h_{n}(r)=\begin{cases}h_{n}^{R_{1}}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\\
h_{n}^{R_{2}}(r),\qquad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}},\end{cases}

and (see (
75
))

\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\left(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2}\right)(r)=\left(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda r\right)\mathsf{g}_{1}(r)+\frac{1}{n}\int_{R_{1}-\varepsilon}^{r}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right),

and (see (
76
))

\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\left(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2}\right)(r)=\left(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda r\right)\mathsf{g}_{2}(r)\\
+\frac{1}{n}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{R_{2}-\varepsilon}^{r}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.\right)\\
-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right).

Remark 7.1
.

Just above and throughout all this section we will write (with a little abuse of notation)
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\left(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2}\right)
and
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\left(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2}\right)
instead of

\mathcal{L}_{n}^{1}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix},\qquad\mathcal{L}_{n}^{2}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\begin{pmatrix}\mathsf{g}_{1}\\
\mathsf{g}_{2}\end{pmatrix},

to alleviate the notation.

Now, we have remembered all the necessary ingredients to obtain the following decomposition:

(179)

\mathcal{L}_{n}^{i}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\left(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2}\right)(r)=I^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{g}_{i}(r)+K_{n}^{R_{i}}(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})(r),\qquad\text{for }i=1,2,

where we defined the auxiliary operator
I^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]:C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})\longrightarrow C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
by the expression

I^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{g}(r):=\left(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r\right)\mathsf{g}(r),

and the operators
K_{n}^{R_{i}}:C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})\times C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})\longrightarrow C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
via the relations

K_{n}^{R_{1}}\left(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2}\right)(r):=\frac{1}{n}\int_{R_{1}-\varepsilon}^{r}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right),

and

K_{n}^{R_{2}}\left(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2}\right)(r):=\frac{1}{n}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{R_{2}-\varepsilon}^{r}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right)\\
-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\left(\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)\mathsf{g}_{2}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right).

On one hand, we shall check that
I^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]:C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})\longrightarrow C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
defines an isomorphism. The continuity of this operator follows from the regularity of the function

(180)

\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r\in C^{\infty}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}),

combined with the fact that
C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
is an algebra.

Proof of (
180
).

Recall (see (
78
) and (
160
)) that

\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r=r^{-1}\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}\left((r-R_{i})\varepsilon^{-1}\right),\qquad\forall r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}.

Considering the change of variables
r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}\mapsto z:=(r-R_{i})\varepsilon^{-1}\in(-1,1)
and applying the fact that we have proved earlier that
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}\in C^{\infty}(-1,1)
does not vanish since

\displaystyle\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)=\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]+O(\varepsilon),\qquad\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)=-\beta(M)\varepsilon+O(\varepsilon^{2}),

we can conclude our result.
∎

Moreover, since
\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r
is not vanishing over
I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}
, one has that
(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r)\text{Id}
is injective. In order to check that such an operator is an isomorphism, it is enough to check that it is surjective, as a consequence of the Banach isomorphism theorem. Take
\mathsf{k}\in C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
, we will find
\mathsf{g}\in C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
such that
(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r)\mathsf{g}(r)=\mathsf{k}(r)
. Indeed,
\mathsf{g}
is given by
(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r)^{-1}\mathsf{k}(r).
Using the regularity of
(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r)
and the fact that it is not vanishing over
I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}
, it is easy to check that its inverse
(\phi^{\prime}(r)+\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}r)^{-1}
still belongs to
C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}).
The fact that
C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
is an algebra implies the desired result.

On the other hand, we have to prove that operator

K_{n}^{R_{i}}:C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})\times C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})\longrightarrow C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})

is compact. To do that, we will prove that

(181)

K_{n}^{R_{i}}:C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})\times C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})\longrightarrow C^{2,\beta}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}),\qquad\beta\in(\alpha,1).

That is, we prove that for any
\beta\in(\alpha,1)
one has the smoothing effect

\|K_{n}^{R_{i}}(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})\|_{C^{2,\beta}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})}\leq C\left(\|\mathsf{g}_{1}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})},\|\mathsf{g}_{2}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})}\right),\qquad\forall(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})\in C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})\times C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}).

In particular, taking
\beta>\alpha
we have that operator

K_{n}^{R_{i}}:C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})\times C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})\longrightarrow C^{2,\beta}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})\hookrightarrow C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})

is compact since the embedding
C^{2,\beta}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})\hookrightarrow C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
is compact.

Proof of (
181
).

First of all, it is important to note that just by definition, we have

K_{n}^{R_{i}}(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})=\mathcal{L}_{n}^{i}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}](\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})-I^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{g}_{i}\in C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}).

Using the relations (see (
67
))

\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)^{\prime}=\mathcal{C}_{n}(r/s)n/r,\qquad\mathcal{C}_{n}(r/s)^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)n/r,

we directly get

(182)

r(rK_{n}^{R_{i}}(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})^{\prime}(r))^{\prime}=r^{2}\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)\mathsf{g}_{i}(r)+n^{2}K_{n}^{R_{i}}(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})(r).

Since
\mathsf{g}_{i},K_{n}^{R_{i}}(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})\in C^{2,\alpha}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
and
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\in C^{\infty}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
, applying directly the fact that Hölder space is an algebra we can conclude that the right hand side of (
182
) belongs to
C^{0,\beta}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}})
.

Therefore, we have proved that
K_{n}^{R_{i}}(\mathsf{g}_{1},\mathsf{g}_{2})\in C^{2,\beta}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}).

∎

Since Fredholm operator remains Fredholm with the same index modulo compact operators and any isomorphism is Fredholm of zero index, we have all the ingredients to deduce (
178
).

Therefore, to apply the bifurcation argument it only remains to check the transversality condition in the Crandall-Rabinowitz Theorem
3.1
. With this in mind, we must first find a practical characterization for the range of the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
.

8.
Adjoint linear operator and their kernel

In order to get a practical and useful characterization for the range
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])
of the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}\equiv\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
we are going to use the adjoint of the linear operator, i.e.,
\mathcal{L}^{\ast}\equiv\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
. Here, it is important to recall and emphasize that
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
will be fixed from the beginning of the section and given by Theorem
6.1
.

Note that, to study the range of the linear operator, we just have to understand the equation

(183)

\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\mathsf{H}(r,\theta),\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon},

with
\mathsf{h}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
and
\mathsf{H}\in Y(D_{\varepsilon})
, where we have assumed that
Y(D_{\varepsilon})=X(D_{\varepsilon})
, see (
26
)-(
27
).

As we did before, we will use the expansions

\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),\qquad\mathsf{H}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}H_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),

which allow us to translate (
183
) into the following infinity-dimensional system

(184)

\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]h_{n}(r)=H_{n}(r),\quad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\cup I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}},

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
with
\mathcal{L}_{n}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
defined via (
73
).

In order to solve (
184
), we will proceed as we did in Section
5
. That is, we re-scale each equation and introduce the auxiliary functions

\bar{H}_{n}^{R_{i}}(z):=(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)H_{n}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z),\qquad\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{i}}(z):=h_{n}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z),\qquad\text{for }z\in(-1,1).

Moreover, we define the auxiliary functional

P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}[\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1},\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}](z):=\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{i}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1}\\
\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}\end{pmatrix}(z),

with
\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
and
\mathcal{L}_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
respectively given by (
81
) and (
82
), for any
\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{1},\bar{\mathsf{g}}_{2}\in C^{2,\alpha}([-1,1]).

Remark 8.1
.

In what follows, to alleviate the notation, we will omit the dependence of
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}.

Therefore, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the functional equation (
184
) is equivalent to solve the system

(185)

z\in(-1,1),\qquad\left\{\begin{aligned} P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}},\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}}](z)&=\bar{H}_{n}^{R_{1}}(z),\\
P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}},\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}}](z)&=\bar{H}_{n}^{R_{2}}(z).\end{aligned}\right.

For convenience, we remind the reader of the explicit expression of the operators on the left-hand side of the above system. That is, for any
\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}\in C^{2,\alpha}([-1,1])
, we have

(186)

P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z)=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)\mathsf{u}(z)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(187)

P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}](z)=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)\mathsf{v}(z)\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{u}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{v}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

with
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)
and
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)
given by (
160
).

8.1.
Analysis in a weak function space

Here, it is important to mention that the analysis about the characterization for the range of the linear operator can be performed in a weighted
L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
space. This weak regularity would be enough to conclude our result.

8.1.1.
Weighted
L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
space

To simplify the notation throughout this section, we begin by introducing two auxiliary weight functions. Note that thanks to the properties (see Lemma
2.6
) of
\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}
, we directly have that

(188)

\sigma_{\pm}(z):=-\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(\pm z)\geq 0,\qquad z\in(-1,1).

Moreover, we introduce the space
L^{2}([-1,1])
with weights
\sigma_{\pm}(z).
That is, we define
L^{2}_{\sigma_{\pm}}([-1,1])
with inner-product and norm given by

(\mathsf{u}_{1},\mathsf{u}_{2})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}:=\int_{-1}^{1}\mathsf{u}_{1}(z)\mathsf{u}_{2}(z)\sigma_{-}(z)\,\mbox{d}z,\qquad(\mathsf{v}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{2})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}}:=\int_{-1}^{1}\mathsf{v}_{1}(z)\mathsf{v}_{2}(z)\sigma_{+}(z)\,\mbox{d}z,

and

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{u}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}

\displaystyle:=(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{u})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}=\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{u}(z)|^{2}\sigma_{-}(z)\,\mbox{d}z,

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{v}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}}

\displaystyle:=(\mathsf{v},\mathsf{v})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}}=\int_{-1}^{1}|\mathsf{v}(z)|^{2}\sigma_{+}(z)\,\mbox{d}z.

In addition, we also introduce the product space
L^{2}([-1,1])\times L^{2}([-1,1])
with weights
\sigma_{-}(z)
and
\sigma_{+}(z)
respectively. That is, we define the Hilbert space

\mathbb{L}^{2}([-1,1]):=L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1]),

with inner-product and norm given by

\langle(\mathsf{u}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{1});(\mathsf{u}_{2},\mathsf{v}_{2})\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}:=(\mathsf{u}_{1},\mathsf{u}_{2})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}+(\mathsf{v}_{1},\mathsf{v}_{2})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}},

and

\|(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}:=\langle(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v});(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}=\|\mathsf{u}\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}+\|\mathsf{v}\|_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}}.

Finally, going back in the re-scaling, we introduce in our original domain
I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}
the space

L_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}):=L_{\tilde{\sigma}_{-}}^{2}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})\times L_{\tilde{\sigma}_{+}}^{2}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}),

with

\tilde{\sigma}_{-}(\cdot):=\varepsilon^{-1}\sigma_{-}((\cdot-R_{1})\varepsilon^{-1}),\qquad\tilde{\sigma}_{+}(\cdot):=\varepsilon^{-1}\sigma_{+}((\cdot-R_{2})\varepsilon^{-1}).

After all this, we are ready to introduce the weaker space
Z(D_{\varepsilon})
given by

Z(D_{\varepsilon}):=\left\{(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}\mapsto\mathsf{g}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}g_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta):\sum_{n\geq 1}\left\lVert g_{n}\right\rVert_{L_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{2}(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}})}^{2}<\infty\right\},

and study the equation

\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\mathsf{H}(r,\theta),\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon},

with
\mathsf{h},\mathsf{H}\in Z(D_{\varepsilon})
.

8.2.
Adjoint operator

Let
\mathbb{L}^{2}([-1,1])=L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1])
be the weighted Hilbert space introduce above and consider the operator:

\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}\right)\colon\begin{array}[]{>{\displaystyle}c @{} >{{}}c<{{}} @{} >{\displaystyle}c}L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1])&\rightarrow&L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1])\\
(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})&\mapsto&\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}],P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}]\right)\end{array}

The main task of this subsection will be to obtain an explicit expression for the adjoint operator. That is, (identifying a Hilbert space with its dual)

\left((P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})^{\ast},(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})^{\ast}\right)\colon\begin{array}[]{>{\displaystyle}c @{} >{{}}c<{{}} @{} >{\displaystyle}c}L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1])&\rightarrow&L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1])\\
(\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g})&\mapsto&\left((P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}],(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}]\right)\end{array}

such that

\langle\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}],P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}]\right);(\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g})\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}}=\langle(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v});\left((P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}],(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}]\right)\rangle_{\mathbb{L}^{2}},

or equivalently

(189)

(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}],\mathsf{f})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}+(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v}],\mathsf{g})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}}=(\mathsf{u},(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}})^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}])_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}+(\mathsf{v},(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}})^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}])_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}}.

It is only a matter of straightforward but tedious calculations, which we leave to the interested reader, to verify that
\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\right)^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}]
and
\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}\right)^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}]
are given by the following expressions:

(190)

\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\right)^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z):=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)\mathsf{f}(z)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(191)

\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}\right)^{\ast}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z):=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)\mathsf{g}(z)\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

8.3.
Kernel of the adjoint operator

The main task of this section is to prove that there exists an element in the kernel of the adjoint operator
\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
. Moreover, we will see that the kernel of the adjoint operator is the span of this element and we will identify the precise form of this element at first order in terms of parameter
\varepsilon
.

The key point to prove our objective will be to use the theory of Fredholm operators. In first place, since
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
is a Fredholm operator, their adjoint operator
\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
is also Fredholm with

\text{index}(\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])=-\text{index}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]).

Moreover, since the linear operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
can be written as an isomorphism plus a compact operator we have (see
[
35
, Theorem 5, p. 641]
) that

\text{dim}(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]))=\text{dim}(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])).

From Theorem
6.1
we can conclude directly that

\text{dim}(\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]))=1.

So far we have shown that there exists
\mathsf{h}^{\ast}\in Z(D_{\varepsilon})
such that
\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\mathsf{h}^{\ast}=0
over
D_{\varepsilon}=I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}\times\mathbb{T}
, which admits an expansion

\mathsf{h}^{\ast}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}h_{n}^{\ast}(r)\cos(n\theta),

satisfying
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]h_{n}^{\ast}=0
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
over
I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}=I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\cup I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}
.

More specifically, using the same convention as before, that is, re-scaling and decomposing the problem, we have proved that there exists
\{\bar{h}_{n}^{\ast}\}_{n\geq 1}\equiv\{((\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}})^{\ast},(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}})^{\ast})\}_{n\geq 1}\in\mathbb{L}^{2}([-1,1])
satisfying

(192)

\begin{cases}\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\right)^{\ast}[(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}})^{\ast},(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}})^{\ast}](z)=0,\\
\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}\right)^{\ast}[(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}})^{\ast},(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}})^{\ast}](z)=0,\end{cases}\qquad z\in(-1,1),

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, with
\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\right)^{\ast}[\cdot,\cdot]
and
\left(P_{n,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}\right)^{\ast}[\cdot,\cdot]
given respectively by (
190
)-(
191
).

In order to identify precisely the form of the unique element in the kernel of the adjoint operator, we will distinguish between two cases:

8.3.1.
Case
n\neq m

Since
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)=O(1)
and
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)=O(\varepsilon)
we can just write

(193)

\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)\mathsf{f}(z)\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0,

and (dividing the full expression by
\varepsilon
)

(194)

\frac{\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{g}(z)\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0.

Hence, we can divide (
193
) by
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)
to get

(195)

\mathsf{f}(z)=-\varepsilon\mathrm{F}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z),

where

\mathrm{F}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z):=(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{1}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{f}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{1}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

The next step will be to introduce (
195
) into the equation (
194
). Hence, we have

(196)

\frac{\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)}{\varepsilon}\mathsf{g}(z)\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathrm{F}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=0.

Now, we introduce the auxiliary functional

(197)

S_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{g}](z):=-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{n}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

which can be written as

S_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{g}](z)=S_{0}[\mathsf{g}](z)+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}S_{\delta})[\mathsf{g}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

with (using (
123
))

S_{0}[\mathsf{g}](z)=-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Then, using (
197
) we have that (
196
) can be written as

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{g}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=-\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\mathsf{g}(z)\varepsilon-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}S_{\delta})[\mathsf{g}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathrm{F}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Introducing a new auxiliary functional
\varepsilon\mathrm{G}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z)
for the right hand side of the above expression, we arrive to

(198)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{g}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(n)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{g}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=\varepsilon\mathrm{G}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z),

with

\mathrm{G}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z):=-\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\mathsf{g}(z)-\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}S_{\delta})[\mathsf{g}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\\
+(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{n}\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\mathcal{S}_{n}((R_{1}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathrm{F}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](s)\,\mbox{d}s,

where
\|\mathrm{G}\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(\varepsilon)
, see (
252
) for details.
Now, applying Lemma
6.18
, we have that solution of (
198
) is given by

(199)

\mathsf{g}(z)=\varepsilon I[\mathrm{G}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}]](z).

The coupled system given by (
195
) and (
199
) is a linear contraction on
\mathbb{L}^{2}([-1,1])
for
\varepsilon
small
enough. Hence, there exists a unique
(\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g})\in\mathbb{L}^{2}([-1,1])
solving (
195
), (
199
):

\displaystyle\mathsf{f}(z)

\displaystyle=-\varepsilon\mathrm{F}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}](z),

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}(z)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon I[\mathrm{G}[\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g}]](z).

Therefore, the trivial solution
(\mathsf{f},\mathsf{g})=(0,0)
is the unique solution of (
193
) and (
194
).

8.3.2.
Case
n=m

To simplify the notation, in the following we will just write

\bar{h}_{m}^{\ast}(z)\equiv\left((\bar{h}_{m}^{R_{1}})^{\ast}(z),(\bar{h}_{m}^{R_{1}})^{\ast}(z)\right)=:(\mathsf{a}^{\ast}(z),\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)).

Taking into account this notation and what we have seen for the case
n\neq m
, we have that the unique element in the kernel of the adjoint operator
\mathcal{L}_{n}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
has the form:

\displaystyle(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{1}})^{\ast}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
\mathsf{a}^{\ast}(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle(\bar{h}_{n}^{R_{2}})^{\ast}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&n\neq m,\\
\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)&n=m,\end{array}\right.

where functions
\mathsf{a}^{\ast}
and
\mathsf{b}^{\ast}
depend on
\varepsilon
,
\kappa
and
m
but we do not make this dependence explicit for sake of simplicity.

To finish this section we need to obtain an explicit expression for
(\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})
at the first order in terms of parameter
\varepsilon
. More specifically, let us see that for some constant C we have

(204)

\begin{cases}\mathsf{a}^{\ast}(z)=\phantom{C\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)}\hskip 14.22636ptO(\varepsilon),\\
\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)=C\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+O(\varepsilon).\end{cases}

Here,
\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)
is given by (
136
). That is, the element in the kernel of the linear operator is exactly the same as (modulo a multiplicative constant) the element in the kernel of the adjoint operator at first order in terms of parameter
\varepsilon.

Remark 8.2
.

The linear operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]
is self-adjoint at first order in terms of parameter
\varepsilon
.

Remark 8.3
.

The information provided by (
204
) will be crucial to prove the transversality property in the Crandall-Rabinowitz’s theorem.

Proof of (
204
).

We start by recalling what we know at this point. That is,
(\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})
solves

\begin{cases}P_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)=0,\\
P_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)=0,\end{cases}\qquad z\in(-1,1).

The above system is linear in
(\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})
and we can assume without loss of generality that

\|\mathsf{a}^{\ast}\|_{L_{\sigma_{-}}^{2}}^{2}+\|\mathsf{b}^{\ast}\|_{L_{\sigma_{+}}^{2}}^{2}=1.

If it is not the case we only need to normalized the solution and enter that value into the final constant
C
. Now, using (
190
) we have that

0=P_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}[\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)\mathsf{a}^{\ast}(z)+O(\varepsilon),

but since we have seen that
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}(z)=O(1)
we get that
\mathsf{a}^{\ast}(z)=\varepsilon\mathsf{a}_{1}^{\ast}(z)
with
\|\mathsf{a}_{1}^{\ast}\|_{L_{\sigma_{-}}^{2}}=O(1)

in terms of
\varepsilon
. This information yields from (
191
) that

0=P_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)=\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)+O(\varepsilon^{2})\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\varepsilon}{m}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Since
\Lambda_{m,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}(z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\varepsilon+\alpha_{2,\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)\varepsilon^{2}
, we divide the above expression by
\varepsilon
to get

(205)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
