-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=O(\varepsilon).

Now, we introduce the auxiliary functional

(206)

V_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z):=-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{1}{m}\int_{z}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon z)/(R_{2}+\varepsilon s))\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\frac{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/(R_{2}+\varepsilon z))}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{m}((R_{2}+\varepsilon s)/r_{1})\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

which can be written as

V_{\varepsilon}[\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)=V_{0}[\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}V_{\delta})[\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)\mbox{d}\delta,

with (see (
123
))

V_{0}[\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)=-\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Then, using (
206
) we have that (
205
) can be written as

(207)

\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)-\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s=O(\varepsilon)-\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}V_{\delta})[\mathsf{b}^{\ast}](z)\mbox{d}\delta:=G(z),

where
\|G\|_{L^{2}([-1,1])}=O(\varepsilon)
, see (
252
) for details. Just dividing (
207
) by
\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)
we have

\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)=\frac{\mathsf{p}_{2}(m)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\frac{G(z)}{\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)}.

Thus, recalling the precise form of
\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)
, given by (
136
) we obtain that

\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)=C\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)+\varepsilon\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\ast}(z),

where
\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\ast}\|_{L_{\sigma_{+}}^{2}}=O(1)
in terms of
\varepsilon
and
C
is a constant.
∎

8.4.
Image of the linear operator

In the previous section we have seen that

\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])=\text{span}\{\mathsf{h}^{\ast}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\},

where
\mathsf{h}^{\ast}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)=h^{\ast}(r)\cos(m\theta)
and with
h^{\ast}(\cdot)
given by

h^{\ast}(r)=\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathsf{a}^{\ast}\left(\tfrac{r-R_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)\qquad&\text{if}\quad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\\
\mathsf{b}^{\ast}\left(\tfrac{r-R_{2}}{\varepsilon}\right)\qquad&\text{if}\quad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}.\end{aligned}\right.

Moreover, we have seen that
(\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})=(\varepsilon\mathsf{a}_{1}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}_{0}+\varepsilon\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\ast})
with
\mathsf{a}_{1}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\ast}=O(1)
in terms of parameter
\varepsilon
.

We also define the orthogonal complement of
(\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})
in
L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1])
:

(\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})^{\perp}:=\left\{(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{v})\in L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}([-1,1])\times L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}([-1,1])\,\mid\,(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{a}^{\ast})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{-}}}+(\mathsf{v},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})_{L^{2}_{\sigma_{+}}}=0\right\}.

Since for this kind of operators, it is well known that
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])=\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}^{\ast}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])^{\perp}
, we are in a good position to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\mathsf{H}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])\subset Z(D_{\varepsilon})
with expansion given by
\mathsf{H}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}H_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta)
. Then, we have that
\bar{H}_{m}\equiv(\bar{H}_{m}^{R_{1}},\bar{H}_{m}^{R_{2}})\in(\mathsf{a}^{\ast},\mathsf{b}^{\ast})^{\perp}
.

Proof.

Direct computation using (
189
).
∎

9.
The transversality property

This section is devoted to the transversality assumption concerning the fourth and last hypothesis of the Crandall–Rabinowitz’s theorem.

Up to this point, we have shown that the linearized operator
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]\equiv D_{\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]
is a Fredholm operator of zero index with one-dimensional kernel. This property is not enough to bifurcate to non-trivial solutions for the nonlinear problem.
A sufficient condition for that, according to Theorem
3.1
, is the transversality property which is equivalent to checking

D^{2}_{\lambda,\mathsf{f}}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]\mathsf{h}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\not\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]),

where
\mathsf{h}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
is the generator (unique modulo a multiplicative constant) of the kernel of
\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}].

Recall that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have proved that there exist an eigenvalue
\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in\mathbb{R}
given by Theorem
6.1
such that
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}])=\text{span}\{\mathsf{h}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\},
where
\mathsf{h}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)=h(r)\cos(m\theta)
and with
h(\cdot)
given by

(208)

h(r)=\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathsf{a}\left(\tfrac{r-R_{1}}{\varepsilon}\right)\qquad&\text{if}\quad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}},\\
\mathsf{b}\left(\tfrac{r-R_{2}}{\varepsilon}\right)\qquad&\text{if}\quad r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}.\end{aligned}\right.

To finish, let us proceed by reduction to absurd. That is, we are going to assume that

D_{\lambda,\mathsf{f}}^{2}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]\mathsf{h}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=rh(r)\cos(m\theta)\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]).

Then, there exists
\mathsf{g}=g(r)\cos(m\theta)\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
such that
\mathcal{L}_{m}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]g(r)=rh(r)
for all
r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}.

Now, applying Lemma
8.4
we have that if
D_{\lambda,\mathsf{f}}^{2}F[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},0]\mathsf{h}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{L}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}[\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}]),
then

\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\mathsf{a}(z)\mathsf{a}^{\ast}(z)\sigma_{-}(z)\,\mbox{d}z+\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\mathsf{b}(z)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)\sigma_{+}(z)\,\mbox{d}z=0.

Taking into account the
\varepsilon
-expansion of each of the terms that appear above and using the fact that
\mathsf{b}_{0}=\mathsf{b}_{0}^{\ast}
we get a contradiction because

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon z)\mathsf{a}(z)\mathsf{a}^{\ast}(z)\sigma_{-}(z)\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle=O(\varepsilon^{2}),

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)\mathsf{b}(z)\mathsf{b}^{\ast}(z)\sigma_{+}(z)\,\mbox{d}z

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon z)|\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)|^{2}\sigma_{+}(z)\,\mbox{d}z}_{O(1)}+O(\varepsilon).

This concludes the last required condition of Crandall-Rabinowitz theorem
3.1
.

10.
Main theorem

In this section we shall provide a general statement that precise Theorem
1.1
and give its proof using all the previous results.

After verifying all the conditions for the application of the Crandall-Rabinowitz Theorem
3.1
and the discussion in Section
2
we obtain the following theorem:

Theorem 10.1
.

Fixed
M>1
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0}(M)
,
\kappa_{0}(M)
such that, for every
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
,
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
m\in\mathbb{N}
with
1\leq m<M
, there exist a branch of solutions
\mathsf{f}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}^{\sigma}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
parameterize by
\sigma
, of equation (
19
), with
|\sigma|<\sigma_{0}
, for some small number
\sigma_{0}>0
,
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
as in Section
2.3
and
\lambda=\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}.
These solutions satisfy:

(1)

\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)
is
\frac{2\pi}{m}-
periodic on
\theta
.

(2)

The branch

\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=\sigma h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+o(\sigma)\quad\text{in }X(D_{\varepsilon}),

and the frequency of the temporal periodicity is

\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}=\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}+o(1),

where
(\lambda_{\varepsilon,\kappa,\sigma},\,h_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m})
are given in Theorem
6.1
and Remark
6.4
.

(3)

\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta)
depends on
\theta
in a non-trivial way.

In addition, the vorticity
\omega^{\sigma}_{\kappa,\sigma,m}\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})
, given implicitly by

(209)

\displaystyle\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})=2A+\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(r),

for
(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})=(r+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta),\theta)
with
\theta\in\mathbb{T}
and
r\in I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\cup I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}
,

(210)

\displaystyle\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})=2A+\varepsilon,

for
\tilde{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}
and
R_{1}+\varepsilon+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(R_{1}+\varepsilon,\tilde{\theta})<\tilde{r}<R_{2}-\varepsilon+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(R_{2}-\varepsilon,\tilde{\theta})
, and

(211)

\displaystyle\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})=2A,

for
\tilde{\theta}\in\mathbb{T}
and either
r_{2}\geq\tilde{r}>R_{2}+\varepsilon+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(R_{2}+\varepsilon,\tilde{\theta})
or
r_{1}\leq\tilde{r}<R_{1}-\varepsilon+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(R_{1}-\varepsilon,\tilde{\theta})
,

yields a time periodic solution for 2D Euler in the sense that

\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta}+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t),\qquad(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}},

satisfies (
10
)-(
11
). Importantly,
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\tilde{r},\tilde{\theta})
depends non trivially on
\tilde{\theta}
.

Then, in order to prove Theorem
1.1
it remains to prove that
H^{\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}-}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})
-norm of
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
can be made as small as we want and that
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,\sigma}\in C^{\infty}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})
. We do this in Theorems
10.2
and
10.4
.

10.1.
Distance of the time periodic solution
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,\sigma}
to the Taylor-Couette flow

The solution
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
obtained in Theorem
10.1
satisfies the following statement:

Theorem 10.2
.

Fixed
M>1
,
0<\kappa<\kappa_{0}
and
0<\gamma<\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}}
, for all
\epsilon>0
and
1\leq m<M
, there exist
\varepsilon>0
and
\sigma>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t)-2A\|_{H^{\gamma}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}<\epsilon.

Proof.

Let us emphasis that we can make
\|\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{C^{2,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}
arbitrarily small fixed
\varepsilon
,
\kappa
and
m
, taking
\sigma
small. We have all the ingredients to obtain a quantitative estimate of the distance between the Taylor-Couette flow and the constructed time periodic solution.

We can compute the homogeneous
H^{\frac{3}{2}-}
norm of
w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t)
using interpolation of Sobolev norms. We introduce the auxiliary function
\tilde{\omega}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot)=\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t)
such that

\|\tilde{\omega}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}-2A\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}=\|\tilde{\omega}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}\lesssim\|\tilde{\omega}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\|\tilde{\omega}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}.

Moreover, as
\left\lVert\tilde{\omega}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{k}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}=\left\lVert\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\right\rVert_{\dot{H}^{k}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}
for
k=1,2
, our problem reduces to

(212)

\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t)-2A\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}\lesssim\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}.

Remark 10.3
.

This way of proceeding will make us lose the independence on
\kappa
. But since we are actually interested on the case
\kappa>0
it will be good enough.

To alleviate the notation let us skip the subscripts
(\varepsilon,\kappa,m)
and the superscript
\sigma
on
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
and
\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
in the rest of the paper. In this section, we will use the notation
{\bf z}=(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}
when it is clear from the context. We will keep
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
and
\varphi_{\kappa}
as we did before.

In order to compute the right-hand side of (
212
), we have (see (
14
) and (
16
)) that

(213)

\nabla\omega(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)=\frac{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)}{1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z})}\left(1,-\frac{\mathsf{f}_{\theta}({\bf z})}{r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z})}\right),\quad\text{on $\supp(\nabla\omega)$},

thus, making the appropriate change of variables, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}^{2}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}}\left|\nabla^{2}\omega({\bf z})\right|^{2}\mbox{d}{\bf z}=\int_{\supp(\nabla^{2}\omega)}\left|\nabla^{2}\omega({\bf z})\right|^{2}\mbox{d}{\bf z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{D_{\varepsilon}}|\nabla^{2}\omega(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)|^{2}(1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z}))\mbox{d}{\bf z}.

Recalling that
\nabla\equiv(\partial_{r},\tfrac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta})
, the expression (
213
) can be written component-by-component as

\displaystyle(\partial_{r}\omega)(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)}{1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z})},

\displaystyle(\partial_{\theta}\omega)(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{f}_{\theta}({\bf z})\frac{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)}{1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z})}.

We are interested in
\nabla^{2}
, that is
\partial_{r}^{2},\tfrac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}^{2}
and
\tfrac{1}{r}\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}-\tfrac{1}{r^{2}}\partial_{\theta}
. Hence, computing second order derivatives we have

(\partial_{r}^{2}\omega)(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)(1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z}))=\partial_{r}\left(\frac{1}{1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z})}\right)\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)+\frac{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(r)}{1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z})},

(\partial_{\theta}^{2}\omega)(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)+(\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}w)(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)\mathsf{f}_{\theta}({\bf z})=\partial_{\theta}\left(\frac{-\mathsf{f}_{\theta}({\bf z})}{1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z})}\right)\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r),

and

(\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\omega)(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)+(\partial_{r}^{2}w)(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)\mathsf{f}_{\theta}({\bf z})=\partial_{\theta}\left(\frac{1}{1+\mathsf{f}_{r}({\bf z})}\right)\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r).

Manipulating the above expression properly we get

\displaystyle\partial_{r}^{2}\omega(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)=

\displaystyle A_{1}({\bf z})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)+A_{2}({\bf z})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(r),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z})|^{2}}\partial_{\theta}^{2}\omega(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)=

\displaystyle A_{3}({\bf z})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)+A_{4}({\bf z})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(r),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z})}\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\omega(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)+\frac{1}{|r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z})|^{2}}\partial_{\theta}\omega(r+\mathsf{f}({\bf z}),\theta)=

\displaystyle A_{5}({\bf z})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(r)+A_{6}({\bf z})\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(r),

where
A_{i}
for
i=1,...,6
are functions which depend on
\partial_{r}\mathsf{f},\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f},\partial^{2}_{r}\mathsf{f},\partial^{2}_{\theta}\mathsf{f},\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}
.

The
C^{2,\alpha}-
norm of
\mathsf{f}
could depend on
\varepsilon
badly. However we always could choose
\sigma
small enough in such a way that the
C^{2,\alpha}
-norm of
\mathsf{f}
is small. Noticing that
C^{2,\alpha}\subset C^{2}
we have that
A_{i}
for
i=1,...,6
are bounded functions.
Then, we have that

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}

\displaystyle\approx\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}\left(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}\right)},

\displaystyle\|\omega\|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}

\displaystyle\approx\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}\left(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}\right)}+\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}\left(I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}\right)}.

Since
I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1},R_{2}}=I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{1}}\cup I_{\varepsilon}^{R_{2}}=[R_{1}-\varepsilon,R_{1}+\varepsilon]\cup[R_{2}-\varepsilon,R_{2}+\varepsilon]
, we just have to study the norms:

\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}([R_{i}-\varepsilon,R_{i}+\varepsilon])},\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{2}([R_{i}-\varepsilon,R_{i}+\varepsilon])}.

Recalling the definitions of
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
and
\varphi_{\kappa}
in section
2.3
. We have for
|r|\leq 1
that

\displaystyle\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}(R_{i}+\varepsilon r)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{i}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\left((-1)^{i}r\right),

\displaystyle\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}(R_{i}+\varepsilon r)

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{-1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\left((-1)^{i}r\right).

Then, after doing the changes of variables
r\leftrightarrow R_{i}+\varepsilon r
and
r\leftrightarrow(-1)^{i}r
we get

(214)

\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}([R_{i}-\varepsilon,R_{i}+\varepsilon])}=\varepsilon\int_{-1}^{1}|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\left(r\right)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}r\leq 2\varepsilon\|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}^{2},

and

(215)

\displaystyle\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}([R_{i}-\varepsilon,R_{i}+\varepsilon])}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{-1}^{1}|\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\left(r\right)|^{2}\,\mbox{d}r.

Since (modulo a normalization factor)

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}_{\kappa}(z)=\int_{-1+\kappa}^{1+\kappa}\Theta\left(\frac{z-\bar{z}}{\kappa}\right)\mbox{d}\bar{z},

thus

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{\kappa}(z)=\frac{1}{\kappa}\left[\Theta\left(\frac{z-(1-\kappa)}{\kappa}\right)-\Theta\left(\frac{z-(-1+\kappa)}{\kappa}\right)\right].

Putting the above expression into (
215
) we get

\displaystyle\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}([R_{i}-\varepsilon,R_{i}+\varepsilon])}\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon\kappa^{2}}\left(\int_{-1}^{1}\left|\Theta\left(\frac{r-(-1+\kappa)}{\kappa}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}r+\int_{-1}^{1}\left|\Theta\left(\frac{r-(1-\kappa)}{\kappa}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}r\right).

Since
\supp(\Theta)\subset(-1,1)
we have that the limits of integration of each integral are
r\in(-1,2\kappa-1)
and
r\in(-(2\kappa-1),1)
, respectively. Note that since
0\leq\kappa<1
we have that
2\kappa-1<1
and both integration domains are contained in
(-1,1)
.
Now, we make the changes of variables
\tilde{r}=r-(-1+\kappa)
in the first integral and
\tilde{r}=r-(1-\kappa)
in the second one. Thus both integrals run from
-\kappa
to
\kappa
:

(216)

\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{\prime\prime}\|^{2}_{L^{2}([R_{i}-\varepsilon,R_{i}+\varepsilon])}\leq\frac{2}{\varepsilon\kappa^{2}}\int_{-\kappa}^{\kappa}\left|\Theta\left(\frac{r}{\kappa}\right)\right|^{2}\,\mbox{d}r\leq\frac{4}{\varepsilon\kappa}\|\Theta\|_{L^{\infty}([-1,1])}^{2}.

Therefore, combining (
214
) and (
216
), there exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t)-2A\|_{\dot{H}^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}^{\frac{1+\gamma}{2}}\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\|_{\dot{H}^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{1+\gamma}{4}}\varepsilon^{\frac{-1+\gamma}{4}}\kappa^{\frac{-1+\gamma}{4}}\leq C\varepsilon^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\kappa^{\frac{-1+\gamma}{4}}.

Moreover, we also have from (
209
)-(
210
)-(
211
) and proceeding as before that

\displaystyle\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t)-2A\|_{L^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}=\|\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}-2A\|_{L^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}\leq\|\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}\|_{L^{2}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}\lesssim\varepsilon.

Consequently, for any
\epsilon>0
and for any
0<s<3/2
, taking
\varepsilon
and
\sigma
small enough, we find a time periodic solution such that its vorticity satisfies

\|w^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(\cdot,\cdot+\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}t)-2A\|_{H^{s}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})}<\epsilon.

∎

10.2.
Full regularity of the solution

This section is devote to proving the following result.

Theorem 10.4
.

The solution
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
in Theorem
10.1
is actually
C^{\infty}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})
.

Proof.

At this point we have proved that
\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
and consequently the vorticity defined implicitly in Theorem
10.1
satisfies that
\omega^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}\in C^{2,\alpha}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})
. In order to improve the regularity, we will use functional equation (
19
), i.e.

\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta))^{2}/2+\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}](r,\theta)-A[\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m},\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}](r)=0,\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}.

with
\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}](r,\theta)=\psi(r+\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}(r,\theta),\theta)
and
\psi
solving (
11
).

Let us remove the superscript
\sigma
and the subscripts
\varepsilon,\kappa
and
m
from
\mathsf{f}^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
and
\lambda^{\sigma}_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}
to alleviate the notation. That is,

(217)

\lambda(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))^{2}/2+\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}](r,\theta)-A[\lambda,\mathsf{f}](r)=0,\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}.

At this point we know that
\mathsf{f}\in C^{2,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
but we will show that in fact the solution belongs to
C^{3,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
. By iterating this procedure, we can finally conclude that the solution
\mathsf{f}
is in
C^{\infty}(D_{\varepsilon}).

The main task of this section will be to upgrade the regularity from
C^{2,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
to
C^{3,\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
. In order to do that, we start recalling that
\mathsf{f}\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
which implies, in particular, that it is mean zero in the angular variable.

So, we can recover
\mathsf{f}
from
\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}
through the expression

(218)

\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)=\text{Int}[\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}](r,\theta):=\int_{0}^{\theta}\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\bar{\theta})\mbox{d}\bar{\theta}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\left(\int_{0}^{\bar{\theta}}\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\tilde{\theta})\mbox{d}\tilde{\theta}\right)\mbox{d}\bar{\theta},\qquad\theta>0.

Taking
\partial_{\theta}
in (
217
) we get

(219)

\lambda(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)+\partial_{\theta}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}](r,\theta)=0,\qquad(r,\theta)\in D_{\varepsilon}.

Since
\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}](r,\theta)=\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)
, we obtain that

\partial_{\theta}\bar{\psi}[\mathsf{f}](r,\theta)=\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)+\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta),

and applying it in (
219
) we get

(220)

\left[\lambda(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))+\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)\right]\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)+\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)=0.

Now we are going to study in detail the terms involved in the above expression, i.e.
\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\circ\Phi[\mathsf{f}]
and
\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\circ\Phi[\mathsf{f}]
with
\Phi[\mathsf{f}](r,\theta)=(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)
. Recall that
\psi[\mathsf{f}]
solves the system

\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta_{B}\psi[\mathsf{f}]&=\omega[\mathsf{f}],\qquad(r,\theta)\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}},\\
\psi[\mathsf{f}]\big|_{r_{1}}&=0,\\
\psi[\mathsf{f}]\big|_{r_{2}}&=\gamma,\end{aligned}\right.

with

(221)

\omega[\mathsf{f}]=\begin{cases}\varpi\circ\Phi_{1}^{-1}[\mathsf{f}],\qquad&(r,\theta)\in\Phi[\mathsf{f}](D_{\varepsilon}),\\
0&(r,\theta)\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\setminus\Phi[\mathsf{f}](D_{\varepsilon}).\end{cases}

Since
\mathsf{f}\in C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
with norm small enough, we have that
\Phi[\mathsf{f}],\Phi^{-1}[\mathsf{f}]\in C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
and as an immediate consequence the “source” term
\omega[\mathsf{f}]
belongs to
C^{2+\alpha}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}).
Applying the classical theory of elliptic regularity, we get
\psi[\mathsf{f}]\in C^{4+\alpha}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})
. Then, we have
\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}],\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\in C^{3+\alpha}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}).

In addition, we can split the first factor of (
220
) in the following way

\lambda(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))+\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)=\left(\lambda r+\phi^{\prime}(r)\right)+\left(\lambda\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)+\left[\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)-\phi^{\prime}(r)\right]\right)

where
\phi^{\prime}(r)=\partial_{r}\psi[0](r)
, see (
61
). Then, remembering that
\phi^{\prime}\equiv\partial_{r}\psi[0]
and taking
\sigma
small enough, we can prove that

(222)

\lambda r+\phi^{\prime}(r)\geq c_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M}>0,\qquad\left\lVert\lambda\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)+\left[\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)-\phi^{\prime}(r)\right]\right\rVert_{L^{\infty}(D_{\varepsilon})}\leq\sigma C_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M},

with

c_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M}-\sigma C_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M}>\frac{c_{\varepsilon,\kappa,M}}{2}>0,

and we can write (
220
) as

(223)

\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)=-\frac{\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)}{\lambda(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))+\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)}.

Hence, we have that
\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}\in C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
. To get extra regularity on the radial variable let us take one derivatives on
r
in (
223
) to obtain

\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)=-\frac{\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)}{\lambda(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))+\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)}(1+\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))\\
+\frac{\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)\left(\lambda+\partial_{r}^{2}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)\right)}{\left[\lambda(r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta))+\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}](r+\mathsf{f}(r,\theta),\theta)\right]^{2}}(1+\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)).

Writing the above expression in a more compact form, we have that

(224)

\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)-\mathsf{K}[\mathsf{f}](r,\theta)\partial_{r}\mathsf{f}(r,\theta)=\mathsf{K}[\mathsf{f}](r,\theta),

with

(225)

K[\mathsf{f}]:=\frac{\left(\lambda\Phi_{1}[\mathsf{f}]+\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\circ\Phi[\mathsf{f}]\right)\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\circ\Phi[\mathsf{f}]-\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\circ\Phi[\mathsf{f}]\left(\lambda+\partial_{r}^{2}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\circ\Phi[\mathsf{f}]\right)}{\left[\lambda\Phi_{1}[\mathsf{f}]+\partial_{r}\psi[\mathsf{f}]\circ\Phi[\mathsf{f}]\right]^{2}}.

Remark 10.5
.

Here, it is important to remind that
K[\mathsf{f}]\in C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
is an immediate consequence of the fact that

\mathsf{f}\in C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
, which implies that
\Phi[\mathsf{f}]\in C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
and
\psi[\mathsf{f}]\in C^{4+\alpha}(\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}})
.

For
\sigma
small enough such that (
222
) holds, we define for
\mathsf{g}\in\{\tilde{\mathsf{g}}\in C^{1+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon}):\int_{\mathbb{T}}\tilde{\mathsf{g}}(r,s)\,\mbox{d}s=0\}
the operator

T[\mathsf{f}]\mathsf{g}:=\mathsf{g}-\mathsf{K}[\mathsf{f}]\text{Int}[\mathsf{g}].

Here it is important to note that
T[\mathsf{f}]
is a compact perturbation of the identity operator since
\mathsf{K}[\mathsf{f}]
has norm as small as we want taking
\sigma
sufficiently small. That is,

\left\lVert\mathsf{K}[\mathsf{f}]\right\rVert_{C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})}\leq\sigma C_{\varepsilon,\kappa,m}.

This is a consequence of the terms
\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}],\partial_{\theta}\psi[\mathsf{f}]
that appear on the numerator (see (
225
)) of
\mathsf{K}[\mathsf{f}]
, which both satisfy that
\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\psi[0]=\partial_{\theta}\psi[0]=0
since
\psi[0](r,\theta)\equiv\phi(r)
.

Thus, for any
F\in C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
there exists the inverse operator
T^{-1}[\mathsf{f}]
, such that, if
\mathsf{g}\in\{\tilde{\mathsf{g}}\in C^{1+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon}):\int_{\mathbb{T}}\tilde{\mathsf{g}}(r,s)\,\mbox{d}s=0\}
satisfies

\displaystyle T[\mathsf{f}]\mathsf{g}=F,

then

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}=T^{-1}[\mathsf{f}]F.

In addition

T^{-1}[\mathsf{f}]:C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})\to C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon}).

Therefore,
\partial_{r}\partial_{\theta}\mathsf{f}
is in
C^{2+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
. Thus, we conclude our goal, i.e., that
\mathsf{f}
belongs to
C^{3+\alpha}(D_{\varepsilon})
.
Finally, we can iterate this process to show that
\mathsf{f}
is in
C^{\infty}(D_{\varepsilon}).

∎

11.
Appendix

In order to facilitate the presentation of the manuscript, we collect in this section all the technical
results and lemmas used previously.

11.1.
Circulation and boundary conditions

Here we remind the reader the very well known conservation of circulation.
Since stream function
\psi
is defined uniquely up to a constant, we can assume without loss of generality that

\psi(r_{1},\theta,t)=0,\qquad\psi(r_{2},\theta,t)=\gamma(t).

Now, we are going to prove that
\gamma(t)\equiv\gamma
is time-independent and that the following relation holds:

(226)

\gamma=\frac{-1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}u_{0}^{\theta}(r,\theta)\,\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta.

Proof of (
226
).

Integrating in angular variable and computing their difference we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma(t)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\psi(r_{2},\theta,t)-\psi(r_{1},\theta,t)\mbox{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}\partial_{r}\psi(r,\theta,t)\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta

\displaystyle=\frac{-1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}u^{\theta}(r,\theta,t)\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta.

The equation for the angular component of the velocity is

(\partial_{t}+u^{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r}u^{\theta}\partial_{\theta})u^{\theta}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}p=0
, which allow us to write

\displaystyle\gamma^{\prime}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}((u^{r}\partial_{r}+\frac{1}{r}u^{\theta}\partial_{\theta})u^{\theta}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}p)(r,\theta,t)\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}u^{r}\partial_{r}u^{\theta}\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta.

Now, applying integration by parts and using the boundary conditions over
\psi
we get

\gamma^{\prime}(t)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}\partial_{r}u^{r}u^{\theta}\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta+\frac{1}{2\pi}\underbrace{\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}\partial_{r}\left(u^{r}u^{\theta}\right)\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta}_{0}.

In addition, using the diverge-free condition, i.e.
\nabla\cdot u=\partial_{r}u^{r}+\frac{1}{r}\partial_{\theta}u^{\theta}=0
, we obtain

\gamma^{\prime}(t)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}\partial_{r}u^{r}u^{\theta}\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}\partial_{\theta}\left(\frac{1}{r}\frac{(u^{\theta})^{2}}{2}\right)\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta=0.

As an immediate consequence
\gamma(t)=\gamma(0)
for all
t\geq 0
and evaluating at initial time we finally obtain that

\gamma=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}\psi_{0}(r_{2},\theta)-\psi_{0}(r_{1},\theta)\mbox{d}\theta=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}\partial_{r}\psi_{0}(r,\theta)\,\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta=\frac{-1}{2\pi}\int_{[r_{1},r_{2}]\times\mathbb{T}}u_{0}^{\theta}(r,\theta)\,\mbox{d}r\mbox{d}\theta.

∎

11.2.
Green’s function to solve
\Phi

The main task of this section will be to solve the system

(227)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta_{B}f(r,\theta)&=g(r,\theta),\qquad(\rho,\theta)\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\\
f(r_{1},\theta)&=0,\\
f(r_{2},\theta)&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Since
g\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
and we look for
f\in X(D_{\varepsilon})
by solving the above. We can, without loss of generality, assume that
f
and
g
admit the following expansions:

f(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}f_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),\qquad g(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}g_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),

where

f_{n}(r)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}f(r,\theta)\cos(n\theta)\mbox{d}\theta,\qquad g_{n}(r)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\mathbb{T}}g(r,\theta)\cos(n\theta)\mbox{d}\theta,

denote the
n-
th Fourier coefficient of the functions
f
and
g
.

Computing the Fourier transform of (
227
) in the angular variable, we have to solve for
r\in[r_{1},r_{2}]
the system

(228)

\left\{\begin{aligned} f_{n}^{\prime\prime}(r)+\frac{1}{r}f_{n}^{\prime}(r)-\left(\frac{n}{r}\right)^{2}f_{n}(r)&=g_{n}(r),\\
f_{n}(r_{i})&=0\qquad i=1,2.\end{aligned}\right.

Solving the ordinary differential equation of (
228
) we get

(229)

f_{n}(r)=K_{1}\cosh(n\log r)+K_{2}\sinh(n\log r)\\
+\frac{\sinh(n\log r)}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\cosh(n\log s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s-\frac{\cosh(n\log r)}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\sinh(n\log s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

with constants values
K_{1},K_{2}
uniquely determined by the boundary conditions.

Now, in order to simplify the notation, we introduce some auxiliary hyperbolic functions

(230)

\mathcal{S}_{n}(r):=\sinh(n\log r),\qquad\mathcal{C}_{n}(r):=\cosh(n\log r).

Moreover, recalling trigonometric identities for hyperbolic functions we have

(231)

\left\{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{S}_{n}(x/y)=\mathcal{S}_{n}(x)\mathcal{C}_{n}(y)-\mathcal{C}_{n}(x)\mathcal{S}_{n}(y),\\
\mathcal{C}_{n}(x/y)=\mathcal{C}_{n}(x)\mathcal{C}_{n}(y)-\mathcal{S}_{n}(x)\mathcal{S}_{n}(y).\end{aligned}\right.

Hence, using (
230
) and (
231
), the expression (
229
) can be written as

(232)

f_{n}(r)=K_{1}\mathcal{C}_{n}(r)+K_{2}\mathcal{S}_{n}(r)+\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Taking into account the boundary conditions of system (
228
), we can translate the problem of determine the values
K_{1},K_{2}
into the following matrix system

\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{C}_{n}(r_{1})&\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{1})\\
\mathcal{C}_{n}(r_{2})&\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}K_{1}\\
K_{2}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\
-\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\end{pmatrix}.

Since by hypothesis
r_{1}<r_{2}
, the matrix of the left is not singular. In fact, using trigonometric identities of hyperbolic functions we get that their determinant is given by

\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2})\mathcal{C}_{n}(r_{1})-\mathcal{C}_{n}(r_{2})\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{1})=\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})\neq 0.

Consequently, the unique solution is computed as follows

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}K_{1}\\
K_{2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2})&-\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{1})\\
-\mathcal{C}_{n}(r_{2})&\mathcal{C}_{n}(r_{1})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0\\
-\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\end{pmatrix}.

That is

(233)

K_{1}:=\frac{-\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\left(-\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right),

and

(234)

K_{2}:=\frac{\mathcal{C}_{n}(r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\left(-\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right).

Therefore, returning to (
232
) and using (
233
) and (
234
) we finally arrive to the following expression

(235)

f_{n}(r)=\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\left(-\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\right)+\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)g_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

11.2.1.
Applying Green’s function to solve the Poisson problem

Now, we have all the ingredients to solve (
62
). That is,

\left\{\begin{aligned} \Delta_{B}\Phi(r,\theta)&=\varpi^{\prime}(r)h(r,\theta),\qquad(\rho,\theta)\in\mathsf{P}_{r_{1},r_{2}}\\
\Phi(r_{1},\theta)&=0,\\
\Phi(r_{2},\theta)&=0.\end{aligned}\right.

Using the expansion

\varpi^{\prime}(r)\mathsf{h}(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\varpi^{\prime}(r)h_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),\qquad\Phi(r,\theta)=\sum_{n\geq 1}\Phi_{n}(r)\cos(n\theta),

and applying (
235
) we obtain directly

\Phi_{n}(r)=\frac{1}{n}\int_{r_{1}}^{r}s\varpi^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/s)h_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s-\frac{1}{n}\frac{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r/r_{1})}{\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\varpi^{\prime}(s)\mathcal{S}_{n}(r_{2}/s)h_{n}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

11.3.
Useful lemmas for the asymptotic analysis of
\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)

In this section we are going to focus on the asymptotic analysis of
\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)
for
i=1,2
. That is,

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)

\displaystyle=(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)\phi^{\prime}(R_{i}+\varepsilon z)

\displaystyle=C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}-A((R_{i}+\varepsilon z)^{2}-r_{1}^{2})-\int_{r_{1}}^{R_{i}+\varepsilon z}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t,

with

\displaystyle C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}

\displaystyle=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)+\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\mbox{d}s\right].

The following computations helps us to obtain the asymptotic expansion of each term of the above expression. First of all, it is sufficient to use the definition of the profile
\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
, recall (
2.3
), to obtain directly that

(236)

\displaystyle\int_{r_{1}}^{R_{1}+\varepsilon z}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t=\varepsilon^{2}\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(-t)\,\mbox{d}t,

and

(237)

\int_{r_{1}}^{R_{2}+\varepsilon z}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t=\varepsilon\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\\
+\varepsilon^{2}\left[\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(-t)\,\mbox{d}t+\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(+t)\,\mbox{d}t-(R_{1}+R_{2})\right].

Much more involved is the asymptotic analysis for the constant value
C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}
given by

\displaystyle C_{\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}

\displaystyle=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)+\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\mbox{d}s\right].

Most of the work will be to analyze the last integral. That is,

(238)

\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\,\mbox{d}s=\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}(\log s)^{\prime}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\,\mbox{d}s\\
=-\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(s)\log s\,\mbox{d}s+\log(r_{2})\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right).

Since the second term was already studied previously in (
237
), we only need to focus on the remaining integral term. That is,

(239)

\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(s)\log s\,\mbox{d}s=\varepsilon\int_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}^{R_{2}-\varepsilon}s\log s\,\mbox{d}s\\
+\varepsilon^{2}\left[\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}(-s)\log(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}(+s)\log(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\,\mbox{d}s\right].

In addition, we have

\displaystyle\int_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}^{R_{2}-\varepsilon}s\log s\,\mbox{d}s

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{s^{2}}{2}\log s-\frac{s^{2}}{4}\right)\bigg|_{s=R_{1}+\varepsilon}^{s=R_{2}-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{(R_{1}+\varepsilon)^{2}-(R_{2}-\varepsilon)^{2}}{4}+\frac{(R_{2}-\varepsilon)^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2}-\varepsilon)-\frac{(R_{1}+\varepsilon)^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1}+\varepsilon),

which can be written, using Taylor’s expansion, as

\int_{R_{1}+\varepsilon}^{R_{2}-\varepsilon}s\log s\,\mbox{d}s=f(\varepsilon)=f(0)+\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}f^{\prime}(\xi)\mbox{d}\xi,

with

f(\xi):=\frac{(R_{1}+\xi)^{2}-(R_{2}-\xi)^{2}}{4}+\frac{(R_{2}-\xi)^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2}-\xi)-\frac{(R_{1}+\xi)^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1}+\xi),

and derivative

f^{\prime}(\xi)=-(R_{2}-\xi)\log(R_{2}-\xi)-(R_{1}+\xi)\log(R_{1}+\xi).

Straightforward calculations imply that (
239
) can be written simply as

(240)

\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}s\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(s)\log s\,\mbox{d}s=\varepsilon\left(\frac{R_{1}^{2}-R_{2}^{2}}{4}+\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})-\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})\right)\\
+\varepsilon^{2}\left[\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}(-s)\log(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}(+s)\log(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\,\mbox{d}s\right]\\
-\varepsilon\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(R_{2}-\xi)\log(R_{2}-\xi)+(R_{1}+\xi)\log(R_{1}+\xi)\mbox{d}\xi.

At this point we have already analyzed all the terms involved in (
238
).
Combining (
237
) and (
240
) appropriately, we obtain that

(241)

\int_{r_{1}}^{r_{2}}\frac{1}{s}\left(\int_{r_{1}}^{s}t\varpi_{\varepsilon,\kappa}(t)\mbox{d}t\right)\,\mbox{d}s=\varepsilon\left[\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\log(r_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})-\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})\right]\\
+\varepsilon^{2}\,\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}.

with

(242)

\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}=\log(r_{2})\left[\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(-t)\,\mbox{d}t+\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(+t)\,\mbox{d}t-(R_{1}+R_{2})\right]\\
-\left[\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}(-s)\log(R_{1}+\varepsilon s)\,\mbox{d}s+\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\varphi_{\kappa}(+s)\log(R_{2}+\varepsilon s)\,\mbox{d}s\right]\\
+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(R_{2}-\xi)\log(R_{2}-\xi)+(R_{1}+\xi)\log(R_{1}+\xi)\mbox{d}\xi.

As an immediate consequence of (
241
) and (
242
) we obtain the expansion

C_{\varpi,\kappa}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)=\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)\right]\\
+\varepsilon\left[\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\log(r_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})-\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})\right]+\varepsilon^{2}\,\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}.

Remark 11.1
.

Note that
\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}=O(1)
in terms of the smallness parameters
\varepsilon
and
\kappa
.

Now, we have all the ingredients to decompose
\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)
as

\Upsilon_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}+\varepsilon\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{i}}(z)+\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z),

with

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{1}}

\displaystyle:=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)\right]-A(R_{1}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{2}}

\displaystyle:=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)\right]-A(R_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{1}}(z)

\displaystyle:=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\log(r_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})-\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})\right]-2AR_{1}z,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(z)

\displaystyle:=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\log(r_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})-\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})\right]-2AR_{2}z-\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2},

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{1}}(z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}{\log(r_{2}/r_{1})}-Az^{2}-\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(-t)\,\mbox{d}t,

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}{\log(r_{2}/r_{1})}-Az^{2}-\left[\int_{-1}^{1}(R_{1}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(-t)\,\mbox{d}t+\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(+t)\,\mbox{d}t-(R_{1}+R_{2})\right].

Remark 11.2
.

Note that
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{i}}(z)=O(1)
for
i=1,2
in terms of the smallness parameters
\varepsilon
and
\kappa
.

Finally, we can make the above expression more manageable by simplifying the above expressions in the following way

(243)

\tilde{\Upsilon}_{0}^{R_{i}}=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\gamma+A\left(\frac{r_{2}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}}{2}-r_{1}^{2}\log\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\right)\right]-A(R_{i}^{2}-r_{1}^{2}),

(244)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{1}}(z)

\displaystyle=\log^{-1}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2}\left(\log(r_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\right)+\frac{R_{1}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{1})-\frac{R_{2}^{2}}{2}\log(R_{2})\right]-2AR_{1}z,

(245)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{2}}(z)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{1}^{R_{1}}(0)-2AR_{2}z-\frac{R_{2}^{2}-R_{1}^{2}}{2},

and

(246)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{1}}(z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}}{\log(r_{2}/r_{1})}-Az^{2}-\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{1}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(-t)\,\mbox{d}t,

(247)

\displaystyle\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{2}}(z)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\Upsilon}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}^{R_{1}}(0)+A(1-z^{2})-\left[\int_{-1}^{z}(R_{2}+\varepsilon t)\varphi_{\kappa}(+t)\,\mbox{d}t-(R_{1}+R_{2})\right],

with
\mathsf{Remainder}_{\varepsilon,\kappa}
given explicitly by (
242
).

11.4.
Functional derivatives of auxiliary terms

In this section we will discuss the technical details to conclude that the following bounds hold:

(248)

\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|,\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|\leq C\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{2}},

(249)

\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|\leq C\left(\|\mathsf{a}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

and

(250)

\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}\left(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{2}\right)[\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon};\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|\leq C\left(|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{a}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right),

where the constant
C
does not depend on neither
\varepsilon,\kappa
or
m
.

Proceeding similarly, we can also prove (see (
162
) and (
170
)) that

(251)

\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}R_{\delta}[\mathsf{v}])(z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|,\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}W_{\delta}[\mathsf{v}])(z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|\leq C\|\mathsf{v}\|_{L^{2}},

and (see (
197
) and (
206
))

(252)

\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}S_{\delta}[\mathsf{v}])(z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|,\left|\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon}(\partial_{\delta}V_{\delta}[\mathsf{v}])(z)\mbox{d}\delta\right|\leq C\|\mathsf{v}\|_{L^{2}},

Proof of (
248
).

We start writing (
109
) and (
113
) in a more convenient way as

\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\lambda_{0}](z)=\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{1}}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1}[\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=\alpha_{0}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{1}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{2}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

with

\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{1}}(\delta,z,s):=(R_{1}+\delta z)(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right).

and

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{1}(\delta,z,s)

\displaystyle:=(R_{2}+\delta z)(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right),

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{2}(\delta,z,s)

\displaystyle:=(R_{2}+\delta z)(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right).

Note that computing their derivative we have

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{1}})(\delta,z,s)=z(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)+m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\right]\\
+s(R_{1}+\delta z)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)-m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\right]

and

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{1})(\delta,z,s)=z(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)+m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\right]\\
+s(R_{2}+\delta z)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)-m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\right],

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{2})(\delta,z,s)=\left[z(R_{2}+\delta s)+s(R_{2}+\delta z)\right]\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)+\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)mR_{2}(z-s).

Therefore,

(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)=-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{1}})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and

(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}](z)=-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{1})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
+\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}^{2})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}_{0}(s)\,\mbox{d}s.

Consequently, we have

\|(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}]\|_{L^{\infty}},\|(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{1})[\mathsf{b}_{0}]\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{2}},

where the constant
C
does not depend on neither
\varepsilon,\kappa
or
m
, and
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}}=\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}([-1,1])}
.
∎

Proof of (
249
).

We start writing (
110
) in a more convenient way as

\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}](z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{a}_{1}(z)+\alpha_{0}^{R_{1}}[\lambda_{0}]\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon}(z)\\
+\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{1}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{2}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{3}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

with

\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{1}(\delta,z,s):=(R_{1}+\delta z)(R_{1}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right),

\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{2}(\delta,z,s):=(R_{1}+\delta z)(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right),

\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{3}(\delta,z,s):=(R_{1}+\delta z)(R_{1}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right).

Note that

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{1})(\delta,z,s)=z(R_{1}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)+m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\right]\\
+s(R_{1}+\delta z)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)-m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)\right],

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{2})(\delta,z,s)=z(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)+m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\right]\\
+s(R_{1}+\delta z)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)-m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\right],

and

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{3})(\delta,z,s)=\left[z(R_{1}+\delta s)+s(R_{1}+\delta z)\right]\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)+\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{1}+\delta z}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)mR_{1}(z-s).

Hence

(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{2})[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}](z)=\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{1})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{2})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{T}_{2}}^{3})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and consequently

\|(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{T}_{m,\delta}^{2})[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}]\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(\|\mathsf{a}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

∎

Proof of (
250
).

We start writing (
114
) in a more convenient way as

\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{2}[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{a}_{2}^{\varepsilon},\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)=\alpha_{1}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1}]\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}(z)+\alpha_{2,\delta}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
+\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{1}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{2}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{3}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{4}(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

with

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{1}(\delta,z,s)

\displaystyle:=(R_{2}+\delta z)(R_{1}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right),

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{2}(\delta,z,s)

\displaystyle:=(R_{2}+\delta z)(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right),

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{3}(\delta,z,s)

\displaystyle:=(R_{2}+\delta z)(R_{1}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right),

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{4}(\delta,z,s)

\displaystyle:=(R_{2}+\delta z)(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right).

Recall also that
\alpha_{2,\delta}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]
is defined by (
140
) as

\alpha_{2,\delta}^{R_{2}}[\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)=\lambda_{0}z^{2}+\lambda_{1}2zR_{2}+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}R_{2}^{2}+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}2zR_{2}\delta+\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}z^{2}\delta^{2}.

Note that computing their derivative we have

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{1})(\delta,z,s)=z(R_{1}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)+m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\right]\\
+s(R_{2}+\delta z)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)-m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)\right],

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{2})(\delta,z,s)=z(R_{2}+\delta s)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)+m\,\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\right]\\
+s(R_{2}+\delta z)\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{r_{1}}\right)\left[\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)-m\,\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{r_{2}}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)\right],

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{3})(\delta,z,s)=\left[z(R_{1}+\delta s)+s(R_{2}+\delta z)\right]\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)+\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{1}+\delta s}\right)m(zR_{1}-sR_{2}),

(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{4})(\delta,z,s)=\left[z(R_{2}+\delta s)+s(R_{2}+\delta z)\right]\mathcal{S}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)+\mathcal{C}_{m}\left(\frac{R_{2}+\delta z}{R_{2}+\delta s}\right)mR_{2}(z-s),

and also

(\partial_{\delta}\alpha_{2,\delta}^{R_{2}})[\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)=2z\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}(R_{2}+\delta z).

Hence

(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{2})[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}](z)=(\partial_{\delta}\alpha_{2,\delta}^{R_{2}})[\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\mathsf{b}_{0}(z)\\
+\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{1})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s-\frac{1}{\mathcal{S}_{m}(r_{2}/r_{1})}\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{2})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s\\
-\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{+1}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(-s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{3})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{a}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s+\frac{1}{m}\int_{-1}^{z}\varphi_{\kappa}^{\prime}(s)(\partial_{\delta}\Phi_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}^{4})(\delta,z,s)\mathsf{b}^{\varepsilon}_{1}(s)\,\mbox{d}s,

and consequently

\|(\partial_{\delta}\mathcal{Q}_{m,\delta}^{2})[\mathsf{a}_{1},\mathsf{b}_{0},\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon},\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}]\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left(|\lambda_{2}^{\varepsilon}|\|\mathsf{b}_{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{a}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathsf{b}_{1}^{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}\right).

∎
